<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that stationary random walks on critical spherical FK–Ising planar maps converge to Liouville Brownian motion on the ordinary unit-area $`\sqrt3`$-quantum sphere, using the geometric and electrical results of the spectral companion. The walk chooses uniformly among all incident half-edges, retaining loops and multiple edges. With the corner measure as the stationary law, the deterministic time acceleration is exactly the number of map edges. For any deterministic metric scale giving the metric-measure limit, convergence retains that same surface and any fixed finite number of conditionally independent walks with their time parameters.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $(M_n,A_n)$ have law <a href="#eq:fk-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fk-law">[eq:fk-law]</a>, and let $a_n>0$ be any deterministic sequence with $a_n\to0$ such that $$\label{eq:assumed-ghp} (V_n,a_nd_n,\mu_n)\ \Longrightarrow\ (S,D_h,\mu_h)$$ in Gromov–Hausdorff–Prokhorov law. Given the map, start $r$ independent simple random walks $X^{n,1},\ldots,X^{n,r}$ independently from $\mu_n$. Given $h$, start $r$ independent Liouville Brownian motions $B^{h,1},\ldots,B^{h,r}$ independently from $\mu_h$, with the convention <a href="#eq:continuum-form" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:continuum-form">[eq:continuum-form]</a>. For every fixed integer $r\ge1$, $$\label{eq:main-limit} \left(\overline M_n,a_nd_n,\mu_n; (\overline X^{n,j}_{nt})_{t\ge0,\,1\le j\le r}\right) \ \Longrightarrow\ \left(S,D_h,\mu_h; (B^{h,j}_t)_{t\ge0,\,1\le j\le r}\right)$$ in the curve-decorated Gromov–Hausdorff–Prokhorov topology.
  - Explanation: Stationary half-edge walks on critical spherical FK–Ising maps converge jointly with their metric and area measure to conditionally independent Liouville Brownian motions, retaining the deterministic edge-count clock.
- **C2** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-graph-energy)): For all real vertex functions $u,v$, $$\label{eq:graph-energy} \mathcal E_n(u,v)=\frac12\sum_{e\in E(M_n)}d_eu\,d_ev.$$ Every parallel edge is a separate summand, every loop has zero summand, and the kernel of $G_n$ consists of the constants.
  - Explanation: The accelerated graph energy is the half-energy sum over edges, with multiplicities retained and loops contributing nothing; connectedness identifies its kernel.
- **C3** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-space-normalization)): Under the metric-measure convergence assumption of the theorem, $\widehat a_n/a_n\to1$.
  - Explanation: The companion diameter normalization is asymptotically equivalent to the assumed deterministic metric scale.
- **C4** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-inputs)): From every subsequence of sizes tending to infinity there is a further subsequence and a representation coupling on which the following properties hold almost surely, simultaneously. The limiting sphere in these coordinates has the ordinary unit-area $\sqrt3$-LQG law, with area $\mu_h$ and metric $D_h$; the latter induces the round topology.* 1. **Geometry in one coordinate.* The vertex measures satisfy $(z_n)_*\mu_n\to\mu_h$ weakly, and $$\begin{aligned} \delta_n&:=\sup_{u,v\in V_n} \lvert a_nd_n(u,v)-D_h(z_n(u),z_n(v))\rvert\longrightarrow0, \label{eq:vertex-distortion}\\ \varepsilon_n&:=d_{\mathrm H}^{D_h}(z_n(V_n),S^2) \longrightarrow0.\label{eq:vertex-density} \end{aligned}$$ The maximum round diameter of the embedded flags tends to zero. Consequently the maximum round diameter of an embedded primal edge tends to zero as well.* 2. **One Hilbert space.* There is an order $v_n(i)$, $0\le i<2n$, of the primal corner occurrences and a continuous tour $\eta:[0,1]\to S^2$ such that $$\label{eq:tour-tracking} \eta_*\,\mathrm dt=\mu_h,\qquad \sup_{0\le t<1} \rho\bigl(z_n(v_n(\lfloor2nt\rfloor)),\eta(t)\bigr) \longrightarrow0.$$ Each vertex $v$ occurs $\deg(v)$ times in the corner list. For $\mu_h$-almost every $z$, the fiber $\eta^{-1}(\{z\})$ contains exactly one time. With $H=L^2([0,1],\,\mathrm dt)$, the maps $$\begin{aligned} J_n:L^2(\mu_n)&\longrightarrow H,& (J_nu)(t)&=u(v_n(\lfloor2nt\rfloor)),\quad 0\le t<1,\\ J:L^2(\mu_h)&\longrightarrow H,& Jf&=f\circ\eta \end{aligned}$$ are isometries, and $J$ is unitary. In particular, $$\label{eq:continuous-pullbacks} \lVert J_n(f\circ z_n)-Jf\rVert_{L^\infty([0,1])}\longrightarrow0 \qquad(f\in C(S^2)).$$* 3. **Centered inverse convergence.* The closed continuum form $\mathcal E_h$ is a regular Dirichlet form and is the form of Liouville Brownian motion with the stated half-energy clock. Its operator $G$ has kernel exactly the constants. For a nonnegative operator whose kernel is the constants, write $A^\dagger$ for its inverse on the mean-zero subspace, extended by zero on constants. Let $\Pi$ be the orthogonal projection onto the constants in $H$, and put $$\label{eq:centered-inverses} K_n=J_n(2G_n)^\dagger J_n^*,\qquad K=J(2G)^\dagger J^*.$$ Thus $K_n$ is also zero on $J_n(L^2(\mu_n))^\perp$. These are bounded positive self-adjoint operators, and $$\label{eq:inverse-convergence} \lVert K_n-K\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}\longrightarrow0.$$* 4. **Local recovery with unit conductivity.* There are ordinary conformal chart domains whose interiors cover $S^2$. Let $V\Subset W\Subset U$ be open sets with $U$ compactly contained in one of these charts, and let $\chi$ be a real smooth function on $S^2$ satisfying $0\le\chi\le1$ and $\operatorname{supp}\chi\subset V$. There are local vertex functions $\widetilde w_n$, defined on graphs containing all incidences needed over $W$, that converge uniformly to $\chi$ on a neighborhood of $\overline W$, in the vertex locations, and satisfy $$\label{eq:local-recovery} \limsup_n\sum_{e:\,z_n(e)\in W} (d_e\widetilde w_n)^2 \le\int_U\lvert\nabla\chi\rvert^2\,\,\mathrm d\mathrm{vol}.$$ Here $z_n(e)$ is any chosen point on the embedded edge, and the integral is the unhalved conformal Dirichlet integral. Every edge is counted with its multiplicity. If one endpoint lies in a fixed compact subset of $W$, then eventually its entire edge, both endpoints, and all the required incidences lie in $W$, uniformly over such edges.* *The assertions in <span class="upright">(iv)</span> hold simultaneously for these buffered tests on the coupled outcome, so the tests may be chosen after fixing that outcome.
  - Explanation: The companion supplies simultaneous geometric, Hilbert-space, inverse-operator, and local recovery inputs on a coupled subsequence. These inputs are imported rather than proved here.
- **C5** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-operators)): For every $s>0$ and $\alpha>0$, the following limits hold in operator norm on $H$: $$\begin{aligned} J_n e^{-sG_n}J_n^*&\longrightarrow J e^{-sG}J^*, \label{eq:semigroup-convergence}\\ J_n\alpha(\alpha+G_n)^{-1}J_n^* &\longrightarrow J\alpha(\alpha+G)^{-1}J^*. \label{eq:resolvent-convergence} \end{aligned}$$ Consequently the finite-dimensional laws of $\xi^n$ converge to those of the stationary Liouville Brownian motion $B^h$.
  - Explanation: Centered inverse convergence gives positive-time semigroup and resolvent convergence, then stationary finite-dimensional walk laws.
- **C6** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-uniform-recovery)): Let $\chi:S^2\to[0,1]$ be smooth, and put $f=1-\chi$. Suppose $\operatorname{supp}\chi\subset V\Subset W\Subset U$ for buffered conformal chart regions to which the local recovery statement of Proposition <a href="#prop:inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:inputs">4</a> applies. There are real vertex functions $w_n$ such that $$\label{eq:uniform-recovery} \lVert w_n-f\circ z_n\rVert_{\infty,V_n}\longrightarrow0, \qquad \limsup_n\mathcal E_n(w_n)\le\mathcal E_h(f).$$
  - Explanation: Buffered local recoveries yield global graph cutoffs with uniform accuracy and no asymptotic excess energy.
- **C7** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-stationary-maximal)): Let $Y$ be a continuous-time Markov chain on a finite state space, with generator $-A$ and reversible probability measure $\pi$, started from $\pi$. For every real function $v$ on that space and every $T>0$, $$\label{eq:stationary-maximal} \mathbb E\sup_{0\le t\le T}\lvert v(Y_t)\rvert^2 \le 2\lVert v\rVert_{L^2(\pi)}^2+40T\langle v,Av\rangle_{L^2(\pi)}.$$ The constants do not depend on the state space or its jump rates.
  - Explanation: Stationarity and reversibility yield a path supremum bound in terms of mass and energy, uniformly over finite chains.
- **C8** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-resolvent-approximation)): For a cutoff $f=1-\chi$ as in Lemma <a href="#lem:uniform-recovery" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:uniform-recovery">6</a>, write $g_n=f\circ z_n$ and define $u_{n,\alpha}$ and $u_\alpha$ by <a href="#eq:resolvent-functions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:resolvent-functions">[eq:resolvent-functions]</a>. For every $T>0$, $$\label{eq:pathwise-resolvent-approximation} \lim_{\alpha\to\infty}\limsup_n \mathbb E\sup_{0\le t\le T} \lvert u_{n,\alpha}(Y^n_t)-g_n(Y^n_t)\rvert=0.$$ For every $n$ and $\alpha>0$, moreover, $$\label{eq:bounded-generator} 0\le u_{n,\alpha}\le1, \qquad \lVert G_nu_{n,\alpha}\rVert_\infty\le\alpha.$$
  - Explanation: Resolvent approximations become accurate along the entire stationary trajectory while their generators remain uniformly bounded at a fixed smoothing parameter.
- **C9** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-path-limit)): On every fixed realization supplied by Proposition <a href="#prop:inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:inputs">4</a>, and for every $T>0$, $$(\xi^n_t)_{0\le t\le T}\ \Longrightarrow\ (B^h_t)_{0\le t\le T} \quad\hbox{in }D([0,T],S^2)$$ for the Skorokhod $J_1$ topology associated with the round metric $\rho$. Every subsequential path limit is continuous.
  - Explanation: Stopping-time control and vanishing edge diameters yield tightness and identify continuous path limits in conformal coordinates.
- **C10** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-ambient-space)): Let $C_n$ be finite connected cable graphs, with vertex sets $V_n$ and all edge lengths equal to $a_n>0$, where $a_n\to0$. Write $d_{C_n}$ for their path metrics. Let $(S,d)$ be a compact metric space, and suppose that maps $z_n:V_n\to S$ satisfy $$\begin{aligned} \delta_n&:=\sup_{u,v\in V_n} \lvert d_{C_n}(u,v)-d(z_n(u),z_n(v))\rvert\longrightarrow0,\\ \varepsilon_n&:=d_{\mathrm H}^{S}(z_n(V_n),S)\longrightarrow0. \end{aligned}$$ There is a compact metric space $(Z,d_Z)$ containing isometric copies of $S$ and every $C_n$, such that, with $c_n=\delta_n/2+a_n$, $$\begin{aligned} \sup_{v\in V_n}d_Z(v,z_n(v))&\le c_n, \label{eq:vertex-bridges}\\ d_{\mathrm H}^{Z}(C_n,S)&\le \max\{a_n+c_n,\varepsilon_n+c_n\}. \label{eq:cable-hausdorff} \end{aligned}$$ If $\nu_n$ is a probability measure on $V_n$ and $(z_n)_*\nu_n\Rightarrow\nu$ on $S$, then $d_{\mathrm P}^{Z}(\nu_n,\nu)\to0$.
  - Explanation: Vertex distortion and density allow a common compact space containing the cable graphs and limiting space isometrically, with vanishing measure discrepancy.
- **C11** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-clock)): Fix a realization supplied by Proposition <a href="#prop:inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:inputs">4</a>, for which <a href="#eq:ambient-path-limit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ambient-path-limit">[eq:ambient-path-limit]</a> holds. Let $X^n$ be the stationary discrete walk on $M_n$, including its selected half-edges, and let $\overline X^n$ be its cable interpolation. Independently of the walk, let $(e_{n,k})_{k\ge1}$ be independent exponential random variables of mean $1/n$, and put $$S_{n,0}=0,\qquad S_{n,k}=\sum_{j=1}^k e_{n,j},\qquad N_n(t)=\max\{k:S_{n,k}\le t\}.$$ Then $Y^n_t=X^n_{N_n(t)}$ has generator $-G_n$ and $Y^n_{S_{n,k}}=X^n_k$. In the ambient space of Lemma <a href="#lem:ambient-space" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:ambient-space">10</a>, for every $T<\infty$, $$\label{eq:clock-comparison} \sup_{0\le t\le T} d_Z(\overline X^n_{nt},Y^n_t)\longrightarrow0 \quad\text{in probability}.$$
  - Explanation: An independent exponential attempt clock, counting loops as attempts, can be removed without changing the limiting cable path law.

```json
      "exact_statement": "Let $(M_n,A_n)$ have law <a href=\"#eq:fk-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fk-law\">[eq:fk-law]</a>, and let $a_n>0$ be any deterministic sequence with $a_n\\to0$ such that $$\\label{eq:assumed-ghp}\n (V_n,a_nd_n,\\mu_n)\\ \\Longrightarrow\\ (S,D_h,\\mu_h)$$ in Gromov–Hausdorff–Prokhorov law. Given the map, start $r$ independent simple random walks $X^{n,1},\\ldots,X^{n,r}$ independently from $\\mu_n$. Given $h$, start $r$ independent Liouville Brownian motions $B^{h,1},\\ldots,B^{h,r}$ independently from $\\mu_h$, with the convention <a href=\"#eq:continuum-form\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:continuum-form\">[eq:continuum-form]</a>. For every fixed integer $r\\ge1$, $$\\label{eq:main-limit}\n \\left(\\overline M_n,a_nd_n,\\mu_n;\n       (\\overline X^{n,j}_{nt})_{t\\ge0,\\,1\\le j\\le r}\\right)\n \\ \\Longrightarrow\\\n \\left(S,D_h,\\mu_h;\n       (B^{h,j}_t)_{t\\ge0,\\,1\\le j\\le r}\\right)$$ in the curve-decorated Gromov–Hausdorff–Prokhorov topology.",
      "explanation": "Stationary half-edge walks on critical spherical FK–Ising maps converge jointly with their metric and area measure to conditionally independent Liouville Brownian motions, retaining the deterministic edge-count clock.",

          "exact_quote": "Let $(M_n,A_n)$ have law <a href=\"#eq:fk-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:fk-law\">[eq:fk-law]</a>, and let $a_n>0$ be any deterministic sequence with $a_n\\to0$ such that $$\\label{eq:assumed-ghp}\n (V_n,a_nd_n,\\mu_n)\\ \\Longrightarrow\\ (S,D_h,\\mu_h)$$ in Gromov–Hausdorff–Prokhorov law. Given the map, start $r$ independent simple random walks $X^{n,1},\\ldots,X^{n,r}$ independently from $\\mu_n$. Given $h$, start $r$ independent Liouville Brownian motions $B^{h,1},\\ldots,B^{h,r}$ independently from $\\mu_h$, with the convention <a href=\"#eq:continuum-form\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:continuum-form\">[eq:continuum-form]</a>. For every fixed integer $r\\ge1$, $$\\label{eq:main-limit}\n \\left(\\overline M_n,a_nd_n,\\mu_n;\n       (\\overline X^{n,j}_{nt})_{t\\ge0,\\,1\\le j\\le r}\\right)\n \\ \\Longrightarrow\\\n \\left(S,D_h,\\mu_h;\n       (B^{h,j}_t)_{t\\ge0,\\,1\\le j\\le r}\\right)$$ in the curve-decorated Gromov–Hausdorff–Prokhorov topology.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-graph-energy",
        "exact_quote": "For all real vertex functions $u,v$, $$\\label{eq:graph-energy}\n \\mathcal E_n(u,v)=\\frac12\\sum_{e\\in E(M_n)}d_eu\\,d_ev.$$ Every parallel edge is a separate summand, every loop has zero summand, and the kernel of $G_n$ consists of the constants.",
        "source_id": "PM-211.1"
      },

          "source_id": "PM-211.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For all real vertex functions $u,v$, $$\\label{eq:graph-energy}\n \\mathcal E_n(u,v)=\\frac12\\sum_{e\\in E(M_n)}d_eu\\,d_ev.$$ Every parallel edge is a separate summand, every loop has zero summand, and the kernel of $G_n$ consists of the constants.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-space-normalization",
        "exact_quote": "Under the metric-measure convergence assumption of the theorem, $\\widehat a_n/a_n\\to1$.",

      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-space-normalization",

        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Under the metric-measure convergence assumption of the theorem, $\\widehat a_n/a_n\\to1$.",
      "explanation": "The companion diameter normalization is asymptotically equivalent to the assumed deterministic metric scale.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-space-normalization",
          "exact_quote": "Under the metric-measure convergence assumption of the theorem,",
          "source_id": "PM-211.1"

      ],
      "id": "C3",
      "kind": "conditional_result",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-space-normalization",
          "exact_quote": "Under the metric-measure convergence assumption of the theorem, $\\widehat a_n/a_n\\to1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-211.1"

      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-space-normalization"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-inputs",
        "exact_quote": "From every subsequence of sizes tending to infinity there is a further subsequence and a representation coupling on which the following properties hold almost surely, simultaneously. The limiting sphere in these coordinates has the ordinary unit-area $\\sqrt3$-LQG law, with area $\\mu_h$ and metric $D_h$; the latter induces the round topology.*\n\n1.  **Geometry in one coordinate.* The vertex measures satisfy $(z_n)_*\\mu_n\\to\\mu_h$ weakly, and $$\\begin{aligned}\n     \\delta_n&:=\\sup_{u,v\\in V_n}\n      \\lvert a_nd_n(u,v)-D_h(z_n(u),z_n(v))\\rvert\\longrightarrow0,\n           \\label{eq:vertex-distortion}\\\\\n     \\varepsilon_n&:=d_{\\mathrm H}^{D_h}(z_n(V_n),S^2)\n           \\longrightarrow0.\\label{eq:vertex-density}\n    \\end{aligned}$$ The maximum round diameter of the embedded flags tends to zero. Consequently the maximum round diameter of an embedded primal edge tends to zero as well.*\n\n\n2.  **One Hilbert space.* There is an order $v_n(i)$, $0\\le i<2n$, of the primal corner occurrences and a continuous tour $\\eta:[0,1]\\to S^2$ such that $$\\label{eq:tour-tracking}\n     \\eta_*\\,\\mathrm dt=\\mu_h,\\qquad\n     \\sup_{0\\le t<1}\n     \\rho\\bigl(z_n(v_n(\\lfloor2nt\\rfloor)),\\eta(t)\\bigr)\n     \\longrightarrow0.$$ Each vertex $v$ occurs $\\deg(v)$ times in the corner list. For $\\mu_h$-almost every $z$, the fiber $\\eta^{-1}(\\{z\\})$ contains exactly one time. With $H=L^2([0,1],\\,\\mathrm dt)$, the maps $$\\begin{aligned}\n     J_n:L^2(\\mu_n)&\\longrightarrow H,&\n     (J_nu)(t)&=u(v_n(\\lfloor2nt\\rfloor)),\\quad 0\\le t<1,\\\\\n     J:L^2(\\mu_h)&\\longrightarrow H,& Jf&=f\\circ\\eta\n    \\end{aligned}$$ are isometries, and $J$ is unitary. In particular, $$\\label{eq:continuous-pullbacks}\n     \\lVert J_n(f\\circ z_n)-Jf\\rVert_{L^\\infty([0,1])}\\longrightarrow0\n     \\qquad(f\\in C(S^2)).$$*\n\n\n3.  **Centered inverse convergence.* The closed continuum form $\\mathcal E_h$ is a regular Dirichlet form and is the form of Liouville Brownian motion with the stated half-energy clock. Its operator $G$ has kernel exactly the constants. For a nonnegative operator whose kernel is the constants, write $A^\\dagger$ for its inverse on the mean-zero subspace, extended by zero on constants. Let $\\Pi$ be the orthogonal projection onto the constants in $H$, and put $$\\label{eq:centered-inverses}\n     K_n=J_n(2G_n)^\\dagger J_n^*,\\qquad\n     K=J(2G)^\\dagger J^*.$$ Thus $K_n$ is also zero on $J_n(L^2(\\mu_n))^\\perp$. These are bounded positive self-adjoint operators, and $$\\label{eq:inverse-convergence}\n     \\lVert K_n-K\\rVert_{\\mathrm{HS}}\\longrightarrow0.$$*\n\n\n4.  **Local recovery with unit conductivity.* There are ordinary conformal chart domains whose interiors cover $S^2$. Let $V\\Subset W\\Subset U$ be open sets with $U$ compactly contained in one of these charts, and let $\\chi$ be a real smooth function on $S^2$ satisfying $0\\le\\chi\\le1$ and $\\operatorname{supp}\\chi\\subset V$. There are local vertex functions $\\widetilde w_n$, defined on graphs containing all incidences needed over $W$, that converge uniformly to $\\chi$ on a neighborhood of $\\overline W$, in the vertex locations, and satisfy $$\\label{eq:local-recovery}\n     \\limsup_n\\sum_{e:\\,z_n(e)\\in W}\n           (d_e\\widetilde w_n)^2\n     \\le\\int_U\\lvert\\nabla\\chi\\rvert^2\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\mathrm{vol}.$$ Here $z_n(e)$ is any chosen point on the embedded edge, and the integral is the unhalved conformal Dirichlet integral. Every edge is counted with its multiplicity. If one endpoint lies in a fixed compact subset of $W$, then eventually its entire edge, both endpoints, and all the required incidences lie in $W$, uniformly over such edges.*\n\n*The assertions in <span class=\"upright\">(iv)</span> hold simultaneously for these buffered tests on the coupled outcome, so the tests may be chosen after fixing that outcome.",
        "source_id": "PM-211.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-inputs",

There are local vertex functions $\\widetilde w_n$, defined on graphs containing all incidences needed over $W$, that converge uniformly to $\\chi$ on a neighborhood of $\\overline W$, in the vertex locations, and satisfy $$\\label{eq:local-recovery}\n     \\limsup_n\\sum_{e:\\,z_n(e)\\in W}\n           (d_e\\widetilde w_n)^2\n     \\le\\int_U\\lvert\\nabla\\chi\\rvert^2\\,\\,\\mathrm d\\mathrm{vol}.$$ Here $z_n(e)$ is any chosen point on the embedded edge, and the integral is the unhalved conformal Dirichlet integral. Every edge is counted with its multiplicity. If one endpoint lies in a fixed compact subset of $W$, then eventually its entire edge, both endpoints, and all the required incidences lie in $W$, uniformly over such edges.*\n\n*The assertions in <span class=\"upright\">(iv)</span> hold simultaneously for these buffered tests on the coupled outcome, so the tests may be chosen after fixing that outcome.",

        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-operators",
        "exact_quote": "For every $s>0$ and $\\alpha>0$, the following limits hold in operator norm on $H$: $$\\begin{aligned}\n J_n e^{-sG_n}J_n^*&\\longrightarrow J e^{-sG}J^*,\n \\label{eq:semigroup-convergence}\\\\\n J_n\\alpha(\\alpha+G_n)^{-1}J_n^*\n &\\longrightarrow J\\alpha(\\alpha+G)^{-1}J^*.\n \\label{eq:resolvent-convergence}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Consequently the finite-dimensional laws of $\\xi^n$ converge to those of the stationary Liouville Brownian motion $B^h$.",
        "source_id": "PM-211.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-operators",
          "exact_quote": "For every $s>0$ and $\\alpha>0$, the following limits hold in operator norm on $H$: $$\\begin{aligned}\n J_n e^{-sG_n}J_n^*&\\longrightarrow J e^{-sG}J^*,\n \\label{eq:semigroup-convergence}\\\\\n J_n\\alpha(\\alpha+G_n)^{-1}J_n^*\n &\\longrightarrow J\\alpha(\\alpha+G)^{-1}J^*.\n \\label{eq:resolvent-convergence}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Consequently the finite-dimensional laws of $\\xi^n$ converge to those of the stationary Liouville Brownian motion $B^h$.",
          "source_id": "PM-211.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every $s>0$ and $\\alpha>0$, the following limits hold in operator norm on $H$: $$\\begin{aligned}\n J_n e^{-sG_n}J_n^*&\\longrightarrow J e^{-sG}J^*,\n \\label{eq:semigroup-convergence}\\\\\n J_n\\alpha(\\alpha+G_n)^{-1}J_n^*\n &\\longrightarrow J\\alpha(\\alpha+G)^{-1}J^*.\n \\label{eq:resolvent-convergence}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Consequently the finite-dimensional laws of $\\xi^n$ converge to those of the stationary Liouville Brownian motion $B^h$.",
      "explanation": "Centered inverse convergence gives positive-time semigroup and resolvent convergence, then stationary finite-dimensional walk laws.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-operators",

      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-operators",
          "exact_quote": "For every $s>0$ and $\\alpha>0$, the following limits hold in operator norm on $H$: $$\\begin{aligned}\n J_n e^{-sG_n}J_n^*&\\longrightarrow J e^{-sG}J^*,\n \\label{eq:semigroup-convergence}\\\\\n J_n\\alpha(\\alpha+G_n)^{-1}J_n^*\n &\\longrightarrow J\\alpha(\\alpha+G)^{-1}J^*.\n \\label{eq:resolvent-convergence}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Consequently the finite-dimensional laws of $\\xi^n$ converge to those of the stationary Liouville Brownian motion $B^h$.",
          "source_id": "PM-211.1"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-prop-operators"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-uniform-recovery",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\chi:S^2\\to[0,1]$ be smooth, and put $f=1-\\chi$. There are real vertex functions $w_n$ such that $$\\label{eq:uniform-recovery}\n \\lVert w_n-f\\circ z_n\\rVert_{\\infty,V_n}\\longrightarrow0,\n \\qquad \\limsup_n\\mathcal E_n(w_n)\\le\\mathcal E_h(f).$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-stationary-maximal",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be a continuous-time Markov chain on a finite state space, with generator $-A$ and reversible probability measure $\\pi$, started from $\\pi$. For every real function $v$ on that space and every $T>0$, $$\\label{eq:stationary-maximal}\n \\mathbb E\\sup_{0\\le t\\le T}\\lvert v(Y_t)\\rvert^2\n \\le 2\\lVert v\\rVert_{L^2(\\pi)}^2+40T\\langle v,Av\\rangle_{L^2(\\pi)}.$$ The constants do not depend on the state space or its jump rates.",
        "source_id": "PM-211.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "source_id": "PM-211.1"
        }

        "exact_quote": "For a cutoff $f=1-\\chi$ as in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:uniform-recovery\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:uniform-recovery\">6</a>, write $g_n=f\\circ z_n$ and define $u_{n,\\alpha}$ and $u_\\alpha$ by <a href=\"#eq:resolvent-functions\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:resolvent-functions\">[eq:resolvent-functions]</a>. For every $T>0$, $$\\label{eq:pathwise-resolvent-approximation}\n \\lim_{\\alpha\\to\\infty}\\limsup_n\n \\mathbb E\\sup_{0\\le t\\le T}\n       \\lvert u_{n,\\alpha}(Y^n_t)-g_n(Y^n_t)\\rvert=0.$$ For every $n$ and $\\alpha>0$, moreover, $$\\label{eq:bounded-generator}\n 0\\le u_{n,\\alpha}\\le1,\n \\qquad \\lVert G_nu_{n,\\alpha}\\rVert_\\infty\\le\\alpha.$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-ambient-space",
        "exact_quote": "Let $C_n$ be finite connected cable graphs, with vertex sets $V_n$ and all edge lengths equal to $a_n>0$, where $a_n\\to0$. Write $d_{C_n}$ for their path metrics. Let $(S,d)$ be a compact metric space, and suppose that maps $z_n:V_n\\to S$ satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\delta_n&:=\\sup_{u,v\\in V_n}\n  \\lvert d_{C_n}(u,v)-d(z_n(u),z_n(v))\\rvert\\longrightarrow0,\\\\\n \\varepsilon_n&:=d_{\\mathrm H}^{S}(z_n(V_n),S)\\longrightarrow0.\n\\end{aligned}$$ There is a compact metric space $(Z,d_Z)$ containing isometric copies of $S$ and every $C_n$, such that, with $c_n=\\delta_n/2+a_n$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\sup_{v\\in V_n}d_Z(v,z_n(v))&\\le c_n,\n \\label{eq:vertex-bridges}\\\\\n d_{\\mathrm H}^{Z}(C_n,S)&\\le\n  \\max\\{a_n+c_n,\\varepsilon_n+c_n\\}.\n \\label{eq:cable-hausdorff}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If $\\nu_n$ is a probability measure on $V_n$ and $(z_n)_*\\nu_n\\Rightarrow\\nu$ on $S$, then $d_{\\mathrm P}^{Z}(\\nu_n,\\nu)\\to0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-211.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-ambient-space",
          "exact_quote": "Let $C_n$ be finite connected cable graphs, with vertex sets $V_n$ and all edge lengths equal to $a_n>0$, where $a_n\\to0$. Write $d_{C_n}$ for their path metrics.           "source_id": "PM-211.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $C_n$ be finite connected cable graphs, with vertex sets $V_n$ and all edge lengths equal to $a_n>0$, where $a_n\\to0$. Write $d_{C_n}$ for their path metrics.       "explanation": "Vertex distortion and density allow a common compact space containing the cable graphs and limiting space isometrically, with vanishing measure discrepancy.",

          "exact_quote": "Let $C_n$ be finite connected cable graphs, with vertex sets $V_n$ and all edge lengths equal to $a_n>0$, where $a_n\\to0$. Write $d_{C_n}$ for their path metrics. Let $(S,d)$ be a compact metric space, and suppose that maps $z_n:V_n\\to S$ satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\delta_n&:=\\sup_{u,v\\in V_n}\n  \\lvert d_{C_n}(u,v)-d(z_n(u),z_n(v))\\rvert\\longrightarrow0,\\\\\n \\varepsilon_n&:=d_{\\mathrm H}^{S}(z_n(V_n),S)\\longrightarrow0.\n\\end{aligned}$$ There is a compact metric space $(Z,d_Z)$ containing isometric copies of $S$ and every $C_n$, such that, with $c_n=\\delta_n/2+a_n$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\sup_{v\\in V_n}d_Z(v,z_n(v))&\\le c_n,\n \\label{eq:vertex-bridges}\\\\\n d_{\\mathrm H}^{Z}(C_n,S)&\\le\n  \\max\\{a_n+c_n,\\varepsilon_n+c_n\\}.\n \\label{eq:cable-hausdorff}\n\\end{aligned}$$ If $\\nu_n$ is a probability measure on $V_n$ and $(z_n)_*\\nu_n\\Rightarrow\\nu$ on $S$, then $d_{\\mathrm P}^{Z}(\\nu_n,\\nu)\\to0$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-clock",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a realization supplied by Proposition <a href=\"#prop:inputs\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:inputs\">4</a>, for which <a href=\"#eq:ambient-path-limit\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:ambient-path-limit\">[eq:ambient-path-limit]</a> holds. Let $X^n$ be the stationary discrete walk on $M_n$, including its selected half-edges, and let $\\overline X^n$ be its cable interpolation. Independently of the walk, let $(e_{n,k})_{k\\ge1}$ be independent exponential random variables of mean $1/n$, and put $$S_{n,0}=0,\\qquad S_{n,k}=\\sum_{j=1}^k e_{n,j},\\qquad\n N_n(t)=\\max\\{k:S_{n,k}\\le t\\}.$$ Then $Y^n_t=X^n_{N_n(t)}$ has generator $-G_n$ and $Y^n_{S_{n,k}}=X^n_k$. In the ambient space of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:ambient-space\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:ambient-space\">10</a>, for every $T<\\infty$, $$\\label{eq:clock-comparison}\n \\sup_{0\\le t\\le T}\n d_Z(\\overline X^n_{nt},Y^n_t)\\longrightarrow0\n \\quad\\text{in probability}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-211.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-clock",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a realization supplied by Proposition <a href=\"#prop:inputs\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:inputs\">4</a>, for which <a href=\"#eq:ambient-path-limit\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:ambient-path-limit\">[eq:ambient-path-limit]</a> holds. Independently of the walk, let $(e_{n,k})_{k\\ge1}$ be independent exponential random variables of mean $1/n$, and put $$S_{n,0}=0,\\qquad S_{n,k}=\\sum_{j=1}^k e_{n,j},\\qquad\n N_n(t)=\\max\\{k:S_{n,k}\\le t\\}.$$ Then $Y^n_t=X^n_{N_n(t)}$ has generator $-G_n$ and $Y^n_{S_{n,k}}=X^n_k$. In the ambient space of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:ambient-space\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:ambient-space\">10</a>, for every $T<\\infty$, $$\\label{eq:clock-comparison}\n \\sup_{0\\le t\\le T}\n d_Z(\\overline X^n_{nt},Y^n_t)\\longrightarrow0\n \\quad\\text{in probability}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-211.1"

      "exact_statement": "Fix a realization supplied by Proposition <a href=\"#prop:inputs\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:inputs\">4</a>, for which <a href=\"#eq:ambient-path-limit\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:ambient-path-limit\">[eq:ambient-path-limit]</a> holds.           "anchor": "stmt-lem-clock",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a realization supplied by Proposition <a href=\"#prop:inputs\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:inputs\">4</a>, for which <a href=\"#eq:ambient-path-limit\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:ambient-path-limit\">[eq:ambient-path-limit]</a> holds. Let $X^n$ be the stationary discrete walk on $M_n$, including its selected half-edges, and let $\\overline X^n$ be its cable interpolation. Independently of the walk, let $(e_{n,k})_{k\\ge1}$ be independent exponential random variables of mean $1/n$, and put $$S_{n,0}=0,\\qquad S_{n,k}=\\sum_{j=1}^k e_{n,j},\\qquad\n N_n(t)=\\max\\{k:S_{n,k}\\le t\\}.$$ Then $Y^n_t=X^n_{N_n(t)}$ has generator $-G_n$ and $Y^n_{S_{n,k}}=X^n_k$. In the ambient space of Lemma <a href=\"#lem:ambient-space\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:ambient-space\">10</a>, for every $T<\\infty$, $$\\label{eq:clock-comparison}\n \\sup_{0\\le t\\le T}\n d_Z(\\overline X^n_{nt},Y^n_t)\\longrightarrow0\n \\quad\\text{in probability}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-211.1"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-lem-clock"
    }
  ],
  "generation_status": "COMPLETE",
  "pm_id": "PM-211.1",
  "schema": "pm.claims.v2"
}
```
<!-- PM:END pm.claims.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Rooted FK configuration space** ([source](#eq:fk-law)): Let $\mathcal F_n$ be the finite set of rooted pairs $(M,A)$, where $M$ is a connected planar multigraph with $n$ edges embedded in the oriented sphere and $A\subseteq E(M)$.
- **Corner measure** ([source](#eq:fk-law)): Write $V_n=V(M_n)$, let $d_n$ be graph distance with every original edge of length one, and let $$\label{eq:corner-measure} \mu_n(v)=\frac{\deg_{M_n}(v)}{2n}.$$
- **Critical spherical FK–Ising law** ([source](#eq:fk-law)): The critical spherical FK–Ising law is $$\label{eq:fk-law} \mathbb P\bigl((M_n,A_n)=(M,A)\bigr) =\frac{2^{\ell(M,A)/2}}{Z_n},\qquad Z_n=\sum_{(M,A)\in\mathcal F_n}2^{\ell(M,A)/2}.$$
- **Dual complement and component count** ([source](#eq:fk-law)): If $M^*$ is the dual map, set $$A^*=\{e^*:e\notin A\},\qquad \ell(M,A)=k(A)+k(A^*)-1,$$ where each component count includes isolated vertices of the corresponding spanning subgraph.
- **Quantum sphere** ([source](#eq:continuum-form)): Let $(S,h)$ be the ordinary unit-area $\sqrt3$-Liouville quantum gravity (LQG) sphere: the quantum-sphere law disintegrated at area one, with its distinguished points forgotten.
- **Liouville Brownian motion** ([source](#eq:continuum-form)): Liouville Brownian motion is the diffusion associated with the closure on $L^2(\mu_h)$ of $$\label{eq:continuum-form} \mathcal E_h(f,f)=\frac12\int_S \lvert\nabla_g f\rvert^2\,\mathrm d\mathrm{vol}_g, \qquad f\in C^\infty(S),$$ where $g$ is any smooth metric in the conformal class of the sphere.
- **Cable interpolation** ([source](#pm-h-3)): For a walk $X^n$, let $\overline X^n$ be its continuous interpolation on the cable realization $\overline M_n$, in which each edge is a unit interval and each selected edge is traversed linearly in one unit of time.
- **Curve-decorated topology** ([source](#pm-h-3)): We use the curve-decorated Gromov–Hausdorff–Prokhorov topology: after isometric embeddings into common metric spaces, the underlying spaces converge in Hausdorff distance, the measures in Prokhorov distance, and every curve uniformly on each bounded time interval.
- **Centered inverse** ([source](#stmt-prop-inputs)): For a nonnegative operator whose kernel is the constants, write $A^\dagger$ for its inverse on the mean-zero subspace, extended by zero on constants.
- **Discrete graph energy** ([source](#pm-h-7)): For a real vertex function $u$, write $\mathcal E_n(u)=\langle u,G_nu\rangle_{L^2(\mu_n)}$, and use polarization for the bilinear form.
- **Arctangent center** ([source](#pm-h-7)): For a finite real random variable $Y$, let $\mathfrak m(Y)$ be the unique zero of $r\mapsto\mathbb E[\arctan(Y-r)]$.
- **Clock modulus** ([source](#eq:continuous-limit-modulus)): For a path $y:[0,T]\to Z$, set $$\omega_T(y,s)= \sup_{\substack{t,t'\in[0,T]\\\lvert t-t'\rvert\le s}} d_Z(y_t,y_{t'}).$$

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-lem-graph-energy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-graph-energy): Normalize the accelerated all-edge graph energy. -> The accelerated graph energy is the half-energy sum over edges, with multiplicities retained and loops contributing nothing; connectedness identifies its kernel.
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-lem-space-normalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-space-normalization): Compare spatial normalizations using continuity of distributional centers. -> The companion diameter normalization is asymptotically equivalent to the assumed deterministic metric scale.
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-prop-inputs) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-inputs): Import a simultaneous subsequence coupling from the spectral companion. -> The companion supplies simultaneous geometric, Hilbert-space, inverse-operator, and local recovery inputs on a coupled subsequence. These inputs are imported rather than proved here.
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-prop-operators) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-operators): Apply continuous functional calculus and identify stationary product expectations. -> Centered inverse convergence gives positive-time semigroup and resolvent convergence, then stationary finite-dimensional walk laws.
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-lem-uniform-recovery) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-uniform-recovery): Clip buffered local recoveries to obtain global cutoffs. -> Buffered local recoveries yield global graph cutoffs with uniform accuracy and no asymptotic excess energy.
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-lem-stationary-maximal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-stationary-maximal): Use forward–backward martingales and the maximal inequality. -> Stationarity and reversibility yield a path supremum bound in terms of mass and energy, uniformly over finite chains.
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-lem-resolvent-approximation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-resolvent-approximation): Pass graph resolvents in the form norm and along the stationary path. -> Resolvent approximations become accurate along the entire stationary trajectory while their generators remain uniformly bounded at a fixed smoothing parameter.
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-prop-path-limit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-path-limit): Apply stopping-time increment bounds and identify continuous subsequential limits. -> Stopping-time control and vanishing edge diameters yield tightness and identify continuous path limits in conformal coordinates.
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-lem-ambient-space) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ambient-space): Join cable graphs to the limiting sphere with short vertex bridges. -> Vertex distortion and density allow a common compact space containing the cable graphs and limiting space isometrically, with vanishing measure discrepancy.
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-lem-clock) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-clock): Compare independent exponential attempt times with deterministic times. -> An independent exponential attempt clock, counting loops as attempts, can be removed without changing the limiting cable path law.
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-clock-2): Combine conditional product limits with convergence of the random environment. -> Stationary half-edge walks on critical spherical FK–Ising maps converge jointly with their metric and area measure to conditionally independent Liouville Brownian motions, retaining the deterministic edge-count clock.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **graph-energy**: The accelerated half-edge walk has graph energy equal to half the sum of edge-difference products; parallel edges retain their multiplicity and loops contribute zero.
- **stationary-path-limit**: Independent stationary walks retain their time parameters and converge jointly with the measured quantum sphere under the stated metric-measure premise and companion inputs.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **arbitrary-start**: Apply the trajectory limit to a walk started at an arbitrary vertex. -> The maximal estimate and path approximation are established for stationary trajectories.
- **geometry-alone**: Infer the time-parametrized walk limit from metric-measure convergence alone. -> Geometry alone does not identify electrical energy or exclude rapid spatial excursions.
- **edge-conventions**: Drop repeated edges or count only vertex-changing attempts when fixing the clock. -> The stationary measure, energy multiplicities, or deterministic attempt clock changes.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The finite graph-energy identity is computable exactly from the edge multiset and vertex functions. This does not certify the asymptotic diffusion limit.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON cases with vertex count, edge multiset and rational functions u,v.
# PRECONDITIONS: Finite connected multigraph, nonempty edges, valid endpoints, finite rational values.
# OUTPUTS: JSON list of exact bilinear graph energies as rational strings.
# TIME: Linear in total edges and vertices, excluding exact arithmetic bit cost.
# SPACE: Linear in input and output size.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact rational arithmetic with python-flint fmpq.
# FAILURE: Invalid inputs raise an exception; no approximate answer is returned.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpq
cases = json.load(sys.stdin)
answers = []
for case in cases:
    k = case['vertices']
    edges = case['edges']
    assert 1 <= k <= 3 and 1 <= len(edges) <= 3
    assert len(case['u']) == len(case['v']) == k
    assert all(0 <= a < k and 0 <= b < k for a,b in edges)
    reached = {0}
    for _ in range(k):
        for a,b in edges:
            if a in reached or b in reached:
                reached.update((a,b))
    assert len(reached) == k
    u = [fmpq(x) for x in case['u']]
    v = [fmpq(x) for x in case['v']]
    total = fmpq(0)
    for a,b in edges:
        du = u[b] - u[a]
        dv = v[b] - v[a]
        total += du * dv
    answers.append(str(total / 2))
print(json.dumps(answers, separators=(',', ':')))
```

```json
    "oracle_code": "import json, sys, hashlib\nfrom fractions import Fraction as Q\nfrom pathlib import Path\nsamples = json.loads(Path(sys.argv[1]).read_text())\nraw = Path(sys.argv[2]).read_bytes()\nobserved = json.loads(raw)\ntruth = []\nfor sample in samples:\n    size = sample['vertices']\n    links = sample['edges']\n    degree = [0] * size\n    adjacency = [[0] * size for _ in range(size)]\n    for left,right in links:\n        degree[left] += 1\n        degree[right] += 1\n        adjacency[left][right] += 1\n        adjacency[right][left] += 1\n    count = len(links)\n    mass = [Q(d, 2 * count) for d in degree]\n    assert sum(mass) == 1\n    transition = [[Q(adjacency[i][j], degree[i]) for j in range(size)] for i in range(size)]\n    assert all(sum(row) == 1 for row in transition)\n    assert all(sum(mass[i] * transition[i][j] for i in range(size)) == mass[j] for j in range(size))\n    first = list(map(Q, sample['u']))\n    second = list(map(Q, sample['v']))\n    applied = [count * (second[i] - sum(transition[i][j] * second[j] for j in range(size))) for i in range(size)]\n    inner = sum(mass[i] * first[i] * applied[i] for i in range(size))\n    truth.append(str(inner))\nassert observed == truth\nassert hashlib.sha256(raw).hexdigest() == sys.argv[3]\n",
    "output_contract": "Exact bilinear graph energy for each input, using the half-energy convention.",
    "reason": "The finite graph-energy identity is computable exactly from the edge multiset and vertex functions. This does not certify the asymptotic diffusion limit.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON cases with vertex count, edge multiset and rational functions u,v.\n# PRECONDITIONS: Finite connected multigraph, nonempty edges, valid endpoints, finite rational values.\n# OUTPUTS: JSON list of exact bilinear graph energies as rational strings.\n# TIME: Linear in total edges and vertices, excluding exact arithmetic bit cost.\n# SPACE: Linear in input and output size.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact rational arithmetic with python-flint fmpq.\n# FAILURE: Invalid inputs raise an exception; no approximate answer is returned.\nimport json, sys\nfrom flint import fmpq\ncases = json.load(sys.stdin)\nanswers = []\nfor case in cases:\n    k = case['vertices']\n    edges = case['edges']\n    assert 1 <= k <= 3 and 1 <= len(edges) <= 3\n    assert len(case['u']) == len(case['v']) == k\n    assert all(0 <= a < k and 0 <= b < k for a,b in edges)\n    reached = {0}\n    for _ in range(k):\n        for a,b in edges:\n            if a in reached or b in reached:\n                reached.update((a,b))\n    assert len(reached) == k\n    u = [fmpq(x) for x in case['u']]\n    v = [fmpq(x) for x in case['v']]\n    total = fmpq(0)\n    for a,b in edges:\n        du = u[b] - u[a]\n        dv = v[b] - v[a]\n        total += du * dv\n    answers.append(str(total / 2))\nprint(json.dumps(answers, separators=(',', ':')))\n",
    "test_vectors": [
      {
        "expected": "3481f213c5175ae61a9cdadffbd1f6bec3875060fbfd214e8e472939f10f97ec",
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | Graph-walk software can adapt the stated transition and energy conventions as consistency checks; the paper does not establish a software performance guarantee. |
| games_simulation | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | A simulation of the stated random map walk can retain stationary initialization, cable interpolation, and the attempted-step clock. The scaling theorem remains conditional on its geometric and electrical hypotheses. |
| tools | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | A graph-energy diagnostic can adapt the exact edge accounting to check a walk generator and expose normalization errors. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The graph-energy identity supplies an exact finite calculation for real vertex functions and a consistency relation for the accelerated walk generator. |
| data_structures | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | The transition rule and energy identity constrain a representation of graph incidences: parallel edges remain distinct and loops retain their half-edges. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT — no accelerator design or hardware execution model is established by the graph and diffusion results. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT — no security property or cryptographic primitive follows from the stationary random-walk limit. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT — the theorem concerns stationary walks on critical spherical FK–Ising maps and supplies no learning objective or training guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT — local energy recovery is an analytic input to path convergence; no optimisation procedure or operations-research guarantee is supplied. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | ABSENT — conditional independence of walks in a shared random environment supplies no distributed protocol or coordination guarantee. |
| other (Probability and random metric geometry) | True | strong | inspirational | stated | The stated result connects electrical energy, operator convergence, and stationary time-parametrized random walks with diffusion on the limiting measured sphere. |

## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **stationary_start** (hypothesis): The path-convergence argument requires stationary initialization; convergence from arbitrary starting vertices is not asserted. Blocked use: ['Use with a deterministic or arbitrary initial vertex without a further result.']
- **companion_inputs** (hypothesis): The path argument uses inverse-operator convergence and uniform energy recovery in addition to metric-measure convergence. Blocked use: ['Infer walk convergence solely from convergence of metric spaces and vertex measures.']
- **external_proofs** (formal_scope): The foundational companion results supplying the common coordinate, conductivity, recovery, and inverse convergence are not proved in this paper. Blocked use: ['Treat the paper as a self-contained proof of the imported geometric and electrical inputs.']
- **all_edge_walk** (hypothesis): The walk uses the whole map and samples incident half-edges uniformly. Blocked use: ['Restrict the walk to the FK configuration.', 'Replace half-edge sampling by uniform sampling of distinct neighbors without establishing the same transition law.']
- **multiplicity** (edge_case): Parallel edges retain their multiplicity in energy, while loops contribute no edge difference. Blocked use: ['Collapse parallel edges when computing the stated energy.', 'Assign a nonzero energy contribution to a loop.']
- **attempt_clock** (edge_case): The clock counts attempted steps, including loop traversals that leave the vertex unchanged. Blocked use: ['Use only changes of vertex as the clock while claiming the stated time normalization.']
- **half_energy** (hypothesis): The continuum diffusion uses the stated half-energy convention; changing this convention changes the time normalization. Blocked use: ['Import an unhalved electrical inverse as though it were the inverse of the Brownian generator.']
- **positive_time** (approximation): The semigroup argument establishes positive-time convergence and does not assert operator-norm convergence at time zero. Blocked use: ['Extend the operator-norm conclusion to time zero.']
- **fixed_walk_family** (hypothesis): The joint convergence statement concerns a fixed finite family of conditionally independent stationary walks. Blocked use: ['Let the number of walks grow with map size without a further uniform result.', 'Replace conditional independence by interacting walks.']
- **buffered_recovery** (hypothesis): Cutoff recovery requires supported smooth tests in buffered conformal chart regions. Blocked use: ['Apply the recovery bound to arbitrary rough tests or unbuffered regions without establishing the necessary hypotheses.']
- **encoding_process** (counterexample_scope): The Brownian paths in the map encoding describe boundary lengths and are different from the vertex walk studied here. Blocked use: ['Identify the boundary-length encoding process with the vertex-walk diffusion.']
- **finite_certificate** (nonconstructive): Checking the finite graph-energy identity does not establish the asymptotic operator and path convergence needed for the diffusion limit. Blocked use: ['Present a finite exact computation as a certificate of the continuum scaling theorem.']
