<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that stationary random walks on critical spherical FK–Ising planar maps converge to Liouville Brownian motion on the ordinary unit-area $`\sqrt3`$-quantum sphere, using the geometric and electrical results of the spectral companion. The walk chooses uniformly among all incident half-edges, retaining loops and multiple edges. With the corner measure as the stationary law, the deterministic time acceleration is exactly the number of map edges. For any deterministic metric scale giving the metric-measure limit, convergence retains that same surface and any fixed finite number of conditionally independent walks with their time parameters.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

| anchor | kind | label | number | first sentence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [stmt-thm-main](#stmt-thm-main) | theorem | `thm:main` | 1 | Let $(M_n,A_n)$ have law [eq:fk-law], and let $a_n>0$ be any deterministic sequence with $a_n\to0$ such that |
| [stmt-lem-graph-energy](#stmt-lem-graph-energy) | lemma | `lem:graph-energy` | 2 | For all real vertex functions $u,v$, |
| [stmt-lem-space-normalization](#stmt-lem-space-normalization) | lemma | `lem:space-normalization` | 3 | Under the metric-measure convergence assumption of the theorem, $\widehat a_n/a_n\to1$.* |
| [stmt-prop-inputs](#stmt-prop-inputs) | proposition | `prop:inputs` | 4 | From every subsequence of sizes tending to infinity there is a further subsequence and a representation coupling on which the following properties hold almost surely, simultaneously. |
| [stmt-prop-operators](#stmt-prop-operators) | proposition | `prop:operators` | 5 | For every $s>0$ and $\alpha>0$, the following limits hold in operator norm on $H$: |
| [stmt-lem-uniform-recovery](#stmt-lem-uniform-recovery) | lemma | `lem:uniform-recovery` | 6 | Let $\chi:S^2\to[0,1]$ be smooth, and put $f=1-\chi$. |
| [stmt-lem-stationary-maximal](#stmt-lem-stationary-maximal) | lemma | `lem:stationary-maximal` | 7 | Let $Y$ be a continuous-time Markov chain on a finite state space, with generator $-A$ and reversible probability measure $\pi$, started from $\pi$. |
| [stmt-lem-resolvent-approximation](#stmt-lem-resolvent-approximation) | lemma | `lem:resolvent-approximation` | 8 | For a cutoff $f=1-\chi$ as in Lemma 6, write $g_n=f\circ z_n$ and define $u_{n,\alpha}$ and $u_\alpha$ by [eq:resolvent-functions]. |
| [stmt-prop-path-limit](#stmt-prop-path-limit) | proposition | `prop:path-limit` | 9 | On every fixed realization supplied by Proposition 4, and for every $T>0$, |
| [stmt-lem-ambient-space](#stmt-lem-ambient-space) | lemma | `lem:ambient-space` | 10 | Let $C_n$ be finite connected cable graphs, with vertex sets $V_n$ and all edge lengths equal to $a_n>0$, where $a_n\to0$. |
| [stmt-lem-clock](#stmt-lem-clock) | lemma | `lem:clock` | 11 | Fix a realization supplied by Proposition 4, for which [eq:ambient-path-limit] holds. |

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

The scaling limit of a random surface does not by itself determine the scaling limit of a walk on that surface. A time-parametrized walk also depends on the electrical energy and on the mass assigned to vertices. We study this additional limit for critical Fortuin–Kasteleyn (FK)–Ising maps, using the common-coordinate and electrical convergence results of the spectral companion (OpenAI 2026) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[SC](#ref-SC)].

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The model and the result

<a id="eq:fk-law"></a>
Let $\mathcal F_n$ be the finite set of rooted pairs $(M,A)$, where $M$ is a connected planar multigraph with $n$ edges embedded in the oriented sphere and $A\subseteq E(M)$. Rooting is at an oriented edge, and pairs are identified by orientation-preserving homeomorphisms preserving the root and $A$. Loops and multiple edges are allowed. If $M^*$ is the dual map, set $$A^*=\{e^*:e\notin A\},\qquad
 \ell(M,A)=k(A)+k(A^*)-1,$$ where each component count includes isolated vertices of the corresponding spanning subgraph. The critical spherical FK–Ising law is $$\label{eq:fk-law}
 \mathbb P\bigl((M_n,A_n)=(M,A)\bigr)
 =\frac{2^{\ell(M,A)/2}}{Z_n},\qquad
 Z_n=\sum_{(M,A)\in\mathcal F_n}2^{\ell(M,A)/2}.$$ Write $V_n=V(M_n)$, let $d_n$ be graph distance with every original edge of length one, and let <a id="eq:corner-measure"></a>$$\label{eq:corner-measure}
 \mu_n(v)=\frac{\deg_{M_n}(v)}{2n}.$$ A loop contributes two to the degree. Conditional on $(M_n,A_n)$, a simple random walk chooses an incident half-edge uniformly and traverses its edge. Its initial vertex has law $\mu_n$, which is stationary. The walk uses all map edges, not just those in $A_n$.

<a id="eq:continuum-form"></a>
Let $(S,h)$ be the ordinary unit-area $\sqrt3$-Liouville quantum gravity (LQG) sphere: the quantum-sphere law disintegrated at area one, with its distinguished points forgotten. Write $\mu_h$ for its area probability measure and $D_h$ for its intrinsic metric in a fixed deterministic normalization. Liouville Brownian motion is the diffusion associated with the closure on $L^2(\mu_h)$ of $$\label{eq:continuum-form}
 \mathcal E_h(f,f)=\frac12\int_S \lvert\nabla_g f\rvert^2\,\mathrm d\mathrm{vol}_g,
 \qquad f\in C^\infty(S),$$ where $g$ is any smooth metric in the conformal class of the sphere. Conformal invariance of the two-dimensional Dirichlet integral makes this definition independent of $g$. The factor $1/2$ fixes the time convention.

For a walk $X^n$, let $\overline X^n$ be its continuous interpolation on the cable realization $\overline M_n$, in which each edge is a unit interval and each selected edge is traversed linearly in one unit of time. We also write $d_n$ for the cable path metric. We use the curve-decorated Gromov–Hausdorff–Prokhorov topology: after isometric embeddings into common metric spaces, the underlying spaces converge in Hausdorff distance, the measures in Prokhorov distance, and every curve uniformly on each bounded time interval.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

<a id="eq:assumed-ghp"></a>
**Theorem 1**. *Let $(M_n,A_n)$ have law <a href="#eq:fk-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fk-law">[eq:fk-law]</a>, and let $a_n>0$ be any deterministic sequence with $a_n\to0$ such that $$\label{eq:assumed-ghp}
 (V_n,a_nd_n,\mu_n)\ \Longrightarrow\ (S,D_h,\mu_h)$$ in Gromov–Hausdorff–Prokhorov law. Given the map, start $r$ independent simple random walks $X^{n,1},\ldots,X^{n,r}$ independently from $\mu_n$. Given $h$, start $r$ independent Liouville Brownian motions $B^{h,1},\ldots,B^{h,r}$ independently from $\mu_h$, with the convention <a href="#eq:continuum-form" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:continuum-form">[eq:continuum-form]</a>. For every fixed integer $r\ge1$, <a id="eq:main-limit"></a>$$\label{eq:main-limit}
 \left(\overline M_n,a_nd_n,\mu_n;
       (\overline X^{n,j}_{nt})_{t\ge0,\,1\le j\le r}\right)
 \ \Longrightarrow\
 \left(S,D_h,\mu_h;
       (B^{h,j}_t)_{t\ge0,\,1\le j\le r}\right)$$ in the curve-decorated Gromov–Hausdorff–Prokhorov topology.*

</div>

Thus the deterministic acceleration can be taken to be exactly $n$. The finite-product assertion retains the conditional walk law in the random environment, rather than only the distribution of one trajectory after averaging over the surface.

The proof uses more than the metric-measure premise <a href="#eq:assumed-ghp" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:assumed-ghp">[eq:assumed-ghp]</a>. The spectral companion supplies a joint conformal coordinate, unit conductivity, uniform local energy recovery, and convergence of centered inverse operators. We state these inputs precisely in Proposition <a href="#prop:inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:inputs">4</a>; their proofs and the foundational companion results on which they depend are not reproduced here. The path-convergence argument from those inputs is proved in full.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### History and related work

Sheffield’s inventory-accumulation encoding related critical FK-decorated planar maps to correlated Brownian paths (Sheffield 2016) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Sheffield2016Inventory](#ref-Sheffield2016Inventory)]. Gwynne and Sun proved that the finite-volume FK contour encoding converges to correlated Brownian motion conditioned to stay in the quadrant and return to the origin (Gwynne and Sun 2015, Theorem 1.8) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[GwynneSun2015Finite](#ref-GwynneSun2015Finite)]. The mating-of-trees theory of Duplantier, Miller, and Sheffield, and its finite-volume sphere form due to Miller and Sheffield, identify the corresponding continuum quantum surfaces (Duplantier, Miller, and Sheffield 2021; Miller and Sheffield 2019) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[DuplantierMillerSheffield2021Mating](#ref-DuplantierMillerSheffield2021Mating), [MillerSheffield2019SphereMating](#ref-MillerSheffield2019SphereMating)]. The Brownian paths in that encoding describe boundary lengths; the vertex walk considered here is a different stochastic process. Liouville Brownian motion was constructed by Garban, Rhodes, and Vargas and by Berestycki (Garban, Rhodes, and Vargas 2016; Berestycki 2015) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[GarbanRhodesVargas2016LBM](#ref-GarbanRhodesVargas2016LBM), [Berestycki2015Diffusion](#ref-Berestycki2015Diffusion)]. Its Dirichlet-form description is developed in (Garban, Rhodes, and Vargas 2014, Theorem 1.7) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[GarbanRhodesVargas2014Dirichlet](#ref-GarbanRhodesVargas2014Dirichlet)].

For mated-CRT maps, Gwynne, Miller, and Sheffield proved quenched walk convergence modulo time parametrization (Gwynne, Miller, and Sheffield 2021, Theorem 3.4) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[GwynneMillerSheffield2021Tutte](#ref-GwynneMillerSheffield2021Tutte)]. Berestycki and Gwynne subsequently retained the parametrization (Berestycki and Gwynne 2022, Theorems 1.2 and 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[BerestyckiGwynne2022MatedCRTLBM](#ref-BerestyckiGwynne2022MatedCRTLBM)], using a deterministic median-exit clock comparable to inverse cell area. Its asymptotic constant is not identified there (Berestycki and Gwynne 2022, Remark 1.3) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[BerestyckiGwynne2022MatedCRTLBM](#ref-BerestyckiGwynne2022MatedCRTLBM)]. The setting here is instead the ordinary finite FK–Ising sphere, the walks start in stationarity, and the limit is joint with the intrinsic metric and area measure. The exact clock in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> rests on the unit-conductivity theorem of the spectral companion (OpenAI 2026, Theorem 5.2) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[SC](#ref-SC)].

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### From operators to paths

The principal issue is tightness with the time parameter retained. Convergence of eigenvalues, or even convergence of the finite-dimensional walk laws, does not exclude rapid excursions of appreciable spatial diameter. The companion’s convergence of centered inverses gives positive-time semigroup convergence. Uniform tracking of the exploration tour, which lists the graph corners and parametrizes quantum area in the limit, then identifies the finite-dimensional distributions in one common coordinate.

To control entire paths, we use smooth spatial cutoffs. Local energy recovery provides uniformly accurate graph cutoffs with asymptotically no excess energy. Resolvent smoothing gives test functions whose generators are uniformly bounded at each fixed smoothing parameter. A reversible forward–backward martingale decomposition converts the form-norm error between these two approximations into an error uniform along a stationary trajectory. Optional sampling of the smoothed cutoffs then gives a stopping-time tightness estimate.

The local contribution is this passage from the companion’s electrical and operator limits to stationary, time-parametrized path convergence. The maximal inequality is a finite-state analogue of the classical Lyons–Zheng forward–backward decomposition (Lyons and Zheng 1988) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[LyonsZheng1988](#ref-LyonsZheng1988)]; we prove the jump-chain identity directly. The last tightness step uses Aldous’s criterion (Aldous 1978) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Aldous1978](#ref-Aldous1978)]. Neither an additional mixing assumption nor a heat-kernel estimate is needed for this passage. Stationarity is essential to the argument as given; we do not assert convergence from arbitrary starting vertices.

Section <a href="#sec:inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:inputs">2</a> records the precise companion inputs and checks the normalization. Section <a href="#sec:operators" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:operators">3</a> obtains the finite-dimensional laws. Section <a href="#sec:tightness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:tightness">4</a> proves the stationary maximal estimate and path tightness. Section <a href="#sec:paths" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:paths">5</a> transfers the limit to cable paths, removes the exponential clock, and assembles the joint convergence in random environments.

<a id="sec:inputs"></a>
### Energy normalization and the common coupling

The proof needs more than the metric-measure limit: it needs convergence of inverse operators and uniform recovery of spatial test functions with the correct energy. We state these inputs from the spectral companion (OpenAI 2026) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[SC](#ref-SC)] precisely, keeping its unhalved electrical convention separate from the Brownian convention of the theorem.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### The graph energy and the spatial scale

Let $P_n$ be the one-step transition operator of the walk, let $L_n=P_n-I$, and put $$G_n=-nL_n=n(I-P_n).$$ Thus $-G_n$ is the generator with total attempt rate $n$, including attempts along loops. It is self-adjoint on $L^2(\mu_n)$. For a real vertex function $u$, write $\mathcal E_n(u)=\langle u,G_nu\rangle_{L^2(\mu_n)}$, and use polarization for the bilinear form. Choose either orientation of each edge and write $d_eu=u(e^+)-u(e^-)$.

<a id="stmt-lem-graph-energy"></a>
<div id="lem:graph-energy" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:graph-energy"></a>
**Lemma 2** (Graph energy). *For all real vertex functions $u,v$, $$\label{eq:graph-energy}
 \mathcal E_n(u,v)=\frac12\sum_{e\in E(M_n)}d_eu\,d_ev.$$ Every parallel edge is a separate summand, every loop has zero summand, and the kernel of $G_n$ consists of the constants.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-graph-energy"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-graph-energy</summary>

*Proof.* Since $\mu_n(x)=\deg(x)/(2n)$, every incident half-edge has $\mu_n$-weighted accelerated rate $1/2$. A nonloop edge with endpoints $x,y$ therefore contributes $$\frac12\bigl(u(x)(v(x)-v(y))+u(y)(v(y)-v(x))\bigr)
 =\frac12d_eu\,d_ev$$ to $\langle u,G_nv\rangle_{L^2(\mu_n)}$. Both half-edges of a loop contribute zero. Summing proves <a href="#eq:graph-energy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:graph-energy">[eq:graph-energy]</a>, including multiplicities. Zero energy forces equality across each edge, hence constancy by connectedness. ◻

</details>

On the limiting conformal sphere, write $\mathcal E_h(f)=\mathcal E_h(f,f)$, also for the closure, and let $G$ be its nonnegative self-adjoint operator. The existence and identification of this closure are part of (OpenAI 2026, Proposition 8.3) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[SC](#ref-SC)]. In the companion’s notation the unhalved operators are <a id="eq:raw-operators"></a>$$\label{eq:raw-operators}
 B_n=2G_n=-2nL_n,\qquad B_h=2G.$$ In particular, the inverses imported below are inverses of $2G_n$ and $2G$, not of $G_n$ and $G$.

The companion fixes space by a deterministic diameter normalization. The next elementary comparison, reproduced from (OpenAI 2026, Lemma 2.3) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[SC](#ref-SC)], shows why its conclusions apply to the sequence $a_n$ in the theorem. For a finite real random variable $Y$, let $\mathfrak m(Y)$ be the unique zero of $r\mapsto\mathbb E[\arctan(Y-r)]$. Set $$\Delta_n=\max\{1,\operatorname{diam}(V_n,d_n)\},\qquad
 \Delta=\operatorname{diam}(S,D_h),$$ and define <a id="eq:diameter-normalization"></a>$$\label{eq:diameter-normalization}
 \widehat a_n=
 \exp\{\mathfrak m(\log\Delta)-\mathfrak m(\log\Delta_n)\}.$$ The expectations defining these centers use the unconditional laws, so $\widehat a_n$ is deterministic.

<a id="stmt-lem-space-normalization"></a>
<div id="lem:space-normalization" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3** (Comparison of spatial normalizations). *Under the metric-measure convergence assumption of the theorem, $\widehat a_n/a_n\to1$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-space-normalization"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-space-normalization</summary>

*Proof.* For every finite real $Y$, bounded convergence shows that $F_Y(r)=\mathbb E[\arctan(Y-r)]$ is continuous, strictly decreasing, and has limits $\pi/2$ and $-\pi/2$ at the two ends of the real line. Its zero therefore exists and is unique. Directly, $\mathfrak m(Y+c)=\mathfrak m(Y)+c$. If $Y_j$ converges in law to $Y$, then $F_{Y_j}(r)\to F_Y(r)$ for each fixed $r$. For each $\varepsilon>0$, the values at $\mathfrak m(Y)-\varepsilon$ and $\mathfrak m(Y)+\varepsilon$ have opposite strict signs. The same eventually holds for $F_{Y_j}$, trapping its zero between these two points. Thus $\mathfrak m$ is continuous under convergence in law.

Diameter is continuous in the Gromov–Hausdorff topology. Since $a_n\to0$, the assumed convergence gives $$a_n\Delta_n\ \Longrightarrow\ \Delta,
 \qquad 0<\Delta<\infty\quad\hbox{almost surely}.$$ Applying the preceding continuity statement after taking logarithms and using the shift identity gives $$\log\frac{a_n}{\widehat a_n}
 =\mathfrak m\bigl(\log(a_n\Delta_n)\bigr)
      -\mathfrak m(\log\Delta)\longrightarrow0.$$ ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### The geometric and analytic inputs

We use $S^2$ for the coordinate sphere and $\rho$ for its round distance. For each occurrence of a vertex, an incident edge, and an incident face, form an equilateral triangle with vertices labelled by these three objects. Repeated incidences remain distinct. Gluing the $4n$ triangles according to the cyclic incidences gives a topological sphere with its piecewise Euclidean conformal structure. Uniformize this surface onto $S^2$, with the normalization of (OpenAI 2026) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[SC](#ref-SC)], and write $z_n:V_n\to S^2$ for the primal vertex locations. The images of the triangles are the embedded *flags*; the primal edges are embedded along their vertex-to-edge-midpoint sides. This common coordinate will retain both the intrinsic metric and the conformal energy.

<a id="stmt-prop-inputs"></a>
<div id="prop:inputs" class="proposition">

**Proposition 4** (Inputs from the spectral companion). *From every subsequence of sizes tending to infinity there is a further subsequence and a representation coupling on which the following properties hold almost surely, simultaneously. The limiting sphere in these coordinates has the ordinary unit-area $\sqrt3$-LQG law, with area $\mu_h$ and metric $D_h$; the latter induces the round topology.*

1.  **Geometry in one coordinate.* The vertex measures satisfy $(z_n)_*\mu_n\to\mu_h$ weakly, and <a id="eq:vertex-distortion"></a><a id="eq:vertex-density"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
     \delta_n&:=\sup_{u,v\in V_n}
      \lvert a_nd_n(u,v)-D_h(z_n(u),z_n(v))\rvert\longrightarrow0,
           \label{eq:vertex-distortion}\\
     \varepsilon_n&:=d_{\mathrm H}^{D_h}(z_n(V_n),S^2)
           \longrightarrow0.\label{eq:vertex-density}
    \end{aligned}$$ The maximum round diameter of the embedded flags tends to zero. Consequently the maximum round diameter of an embedded primal edge tends to zero as well.*

<a id="eq:continuous-pullbacks"></a>
2.  **One Hilbert space.* There is an order $v_n(i)$, $0\le i<2n$, of the primal corner occurrences and a continuous tour $\eta:[0,1]\to S^2$ such that <a id="eq:tour-tracking"></a>$$\label{eq:tour-tracking}
     \eta_*\,\mathrm dt=\mu_h,\qquad
     \sup_{0\le t<1}
     \rho\bigl(z_n(v_n(\lfloor2nt\rfloor)),\eta(t)\bigr)
     \longrightarrow0.$$ Each vertex $v$ occurs $\deg(v)$ times in the corner list. For $\mu_h$-almost every $z$, the fiber $\eta^{-1}(\{z\})$ contains exactly one time. With $H=L^2([0,1],\,\mathrm dt)$, the maps $$\begin{aligned}
     J_n:L^2(\mu_n)&\longrightarrow H,&
     (J_nu)(t)&=u(v_n(\lfloor2nt\rfloor)),\quad 0\le t<1,\\
     J:L^2(\mu_h)&\longrightarrow H,& Jf&=f\circ\eta
    \end{aligned}$$ are isometries, and $J$ is unitary. In particular, $$\label{eq:continuous-pullbacks}
     \lVert J_n(f\circ z_n)-Jf\rVert_{L^\infty([0,1])}\longrightarrow0
     \qquad(f\in C(S^2)).$$*

<a id="eq:centered-inverses"></a>
3.  **Centered inverse convergence.* The closed continuum form $\mathcal E_h$ is a regular Dirichlet form and is the form of Liouville Brownian motion with the stated half-energy clock. Its operator $G$ has kernel exactly the constants. For a nonnegative operator whose kernel is the constants, write $A^\dagger$ for its inverse on the mean-zero subspace, extended by zero on constants. Let $\Pi$ be the orthogonal projection onto the constants in $H$, and put $$\label{eq:centered-inverses}
     K_n=J_n(2G_n)^\dagger J_n^*,\qquad
     K=J(2G)^\dagger J^*.$$ Thus $K_n$ is also zero on $J_n(L^2(\mu_n))^\perp$. These are bounded positive self-adjoint operators, and <a id="eq:inverse-convergence"></a>$$\label{eq:inverse-convergence}
     \lVert K_n-K\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}\longrightarrow0.$$*

<a id="eq:local-recovery"></a>
4.  **Local recovery with unit conductivity.* There are ordinary conformal chart domains whose interiors cover $S^2$. Let $V\Subset W\Subset U$ be open sets with $U$ compactly contained in one of these charts, and let $\chi$ be a real smooth function on $S^2$ satisfying $0\le\chi\le1$ and $\operatorname{supp}\chi\subset V$. There are local vertex functions $\widetilde w_n$, defined on graphs containing all incidences needed over $W$, that converge uniformly to $\chi$ on a neighborhood of $\overline W$, in the vertex locations, and satisfy $$\label{eq:local-recovery}
     \limsup_n\sum_{e:\,z_n(e)\in W}
           (d_e\widetilde w_n)^2
     \le\int_U\lvert\nabla\chi\rvert^2\,\,\mathrm d\mathrm{vol}.$$ Here $z_n(e)$ is any chosen point on the embedded edge, and the integral is the unhalved conformal Dirichlet integral. Every edge is counted with its multiplicity. If one endpoint lies in a fixed compact subset of $W$, then eventually its entire edge, both endpoints, and all the required incidences lie in $W$, uniformly over such edges.*

*The assertions in <span class="upright">(iv)</span> hold simultaneously for these buffered tests on the coupled outcome, so the tests may be chosen after fixing that outcome.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-inputs"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-inputs</summary>

*Source of the inputs and comparison of conventions.* The substantial assertions are imported from (OpenAI 2026) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[SC](#ref-SC)]. Its Proposition 2.4 supplies the common coordinate, the full flag mesh, and the tours; its Theorem 5.2 supplies the local recovery assertion; its Proposition 8.3 identifies the closed continuum form and centered inverse; and its Lemma 9.1 and Equation (9.11) give the Hilbert-space identification and Hilbert–Schmidt convergence. In the common extraction of its Section 9, retain also the countable family of local constrained-energy infima from its Lemma 5.1. These are infima of finite sums of squared edge differences under rational interval constraints on vertex values, viewed in the compact space $[0,\infty]$. Their joint limits supply the simultaneous recovery statement of Theorem 5.2, and persist on further subsequences. Proposition 2.4 preserves the geometric conclusions under this joint extraction, so all four assertions hold on one coupling.

The model conventions agree exactly: the companion uses the same rooted FK weight $2^{\ell/2}$, counts all $n$ primal edges, and pushes normalized corner counting to $\deg(v)/(2n)$. Its all-edge network therefore has operator $B_n=2G_n$ by Lemma <a href="#lem:graph-energy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:graph-energy">2</a>. Its limiting surface is the ordinary unit-area sphere with the original marks forgotten. Auxiliary marks and rerootings used in the coupling change neither of these marginals.

For completeness, the companion first gives Hausdorff convergence of the distance graphs $$\{(z_n(u),z_n(v),\widehat a_nd_n(u,v)):u,v\in V_n\}
 \longrightarrow
 \{(x,y,D_h(x,y)):x,y\in S^2\}$$ in the product of the round topology and the ordinary topology on $[0,\infty)$. Uniform continuity of $D_h$ on the compact $S^2\times S^2$ turns this into vanishing vertex distortion. Approximating the triples $(x,x,0)$ in the opposite direction gives round density of the vertex locations, hence $D_h$-density. Lemma <a href="#lem:space-normalization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:space-normalization">3</a> permits replacement of $\widehat a_n$ by $a_n$: along this coupling the $\widehat a_n$-scaled diameters are bounded, and the ratio of the two scales tends to one.

The corner multiplicities give the isometry of $J_n$, and the area pushforward gives that of $J$. The almost-everywhere singleton-fiber property makes $J$ onto, as detailed in (OpenAI 2026, Lemma 9.1) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[SC](#ref-SC)]. Uniform tour tracking and uniform continuity of $f$ give <a href="#eq:continuous-pullbacks" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:continuous-pullbacks">[eq:continuous-pullbacks]</a>. The centered inverses in the companion are those of the unhalved operators, which yields exactly <a href="#eq:centered-inverses" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:centered-inverses">[eq:centered-inverses]</a>.

Finally, the chart families in (OpenAI 2026, Proposition 2.4(iv)) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[SC](#ref-SC)] use two distinct exploration roots and separate auxiliary mark pairs. Their interiors cover the entire sphere, not merely the complement of one root. The finite-sphere clause of its Theorem 5.2 gives <a href="#eq:local-recovery" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:local-recovery">[eq:local-recovery]</a> in the common coordinate, simultaneously for continuous finite-energy inputs and buffered domains. We have stated only the smooth supported case needed here. The vanishing flag mesh and the strict buffers ensure that edges incident at the compact support are indeed among the edges counted in $W$. Conformal and anticonformal coordinate changes preserve the Dirichlet integral, and hence the diffusion and its clock. ◻

</details>

Fix henceforth one outcome in the probability-one event of Proposition <a href="#prop:inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:inputs">4</a>, and relabel its further subsequence by $n$. Until we return to random environments, all graphs, coordinates, and the limiting measured conformal sphere are fixed. The symbols $\mathbb P$ and $\mathbb E$ will refer to the law and expectation of walks sampled with fresh randomness on these fixed graphs, started from $\mu_n$, or of the limiting motion started from $\mu_h$.

<a id="sec:operators"></a>
### Operators and finite-dimensional laws

Fix a realization in the probability-one event of Proposition <a href="#prop:inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:inputs">4</a>, and retain its extracted sequence. All limits in this section and the next are on these fixed measured graphs and this fixed measured sphere. Let $Y^n$ be the stationary chain with generator $-G_n$, and set $\xi^n_t=z_n(Y^n_t)$. We first identify its finite-dimensional limits. The centered inverses contain the required positive-time information, provided that the constants and the orthogonal complements of the discrete spaces are treated separately.

<a id="stmt-prop-operators"></a>
<div id="prop:operators" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:semigroup-convergence"></a>
<a id="eq:resolvent-convergence"></a>
**Proposition 5**. *For every $s>0$ and $\alpha>0$, the following limits hold in operator norm on $H$: $$\begin{aligned}
 J_n e^{-sG_n}J_n^*&\longrightarrow J e^{-sG}J^*,
 \label{eq:semigroup-convergence}\\
 J_n\alpha(\alpha+G_n)^{-1}J_n^*
 &\longrightarrow J\alpha(\alpha+G)^{-1}J^*.
 \label{eq:resolvent-convergence}
\end{aligned}$$ Consequently the finite-dimensional laws of $\xi^n$ converge to those of the stationary Liouville Brownian motion $B^h$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-operators"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-operators</summary>

*Proof.* Write $\Pi$ for the orthogonal projection onto the constants in $H$. On the mean-zero part of $J_nL^2(\mu_n)$, the operator $2K_n$ is the inverse of $G_n$. On the constants and on $(J_nL^2(\mu_n))^\perp$, it is zero. Therefore <a id="eq:functional-calculus"></a>$$\label{eq:functional-calculus}
 J_n e^{-sG_n}J_n^*=\Pi+\psi_s(2K_n),
 \qquad
 \psi_s(y)=
 \begin{cases}e^{-s/y},&y>0,\\0,&y=0.\end{cases}$$ In particular, both sides vanish on the orthogonal complement of the discrete space, while both act as the identity on constants. The same identity holds for $G$ and $K$, with no extra orthogonal complement since $J$ is unitary. Hilbert–Schmidt convergence of $K_n$ implies operator-norm convergence and places all their spectra in a common bounded interval. Since $\psi_s$ is continuous on that interval, continuous functional calculus proves <a href="#eq:semigroup-convergence" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:semigroup-convergence">[eq:semigroup-convergence]</a>. Similarly, $$J_n\alpha(\alpha+G_n)^{-1}J_n^*
   =\Pi+r_\alpha(2K_n),
 \qquad r_\alpha(y)=\frac{\alpha y}{1+\alpha y},$$ and the corresponding identity for $G$ proves <a href="#eq:resolvent-convergence" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:resolvent-convergence">[eq:resolvent-convergence]</a>. Neither argument asserts operator-norm convergence at time zero.

<a id="eq:fdd-products"></a>
To identify finite-dimensional laws, take continuous real functions $f_0,\ldots,f_k$ on $S^2$ and times $0\le t_0<t_1<\cdots<t_k$. Put $g_{n,i}=f_i\circ z_n$ and let $M_a$ denote multiplication by a bounded function $a$ on $H$. By stationarity and the Markov property, $$\label{eq:fdd-products}
 \mathbb E\prod_{i=0}^k f_i(\xi^n_{t_i})
 =\big\langle 1,
 M_{J_ng_{n,0}}S_n(t_1-t_0)M_{J_ng_{n,1}}
 \cdots S_n(t_k-t_{k-1})M_{J_ng_{n,k}}1\big\rangle_H,$$ where $S_n(s)=J_ne^{-sG_n}J_n^*$. Indeed, multiplication by $J_ng_{n,i}$ preserves the range of $J_n$ and represents multiplication by $g_{n,i}$ there. The tour convergence in Proposition <a href="#prop:inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:inputs">4</a> gives $J_ng_{n,i}\to Jf_i$ uniformly, hence convergence of these multiplication operators in norm. Equation <a href="#eq:semigroup-convergence" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:semigroup-convergence">[eq:semigroup-convergence]</a> now passes every factor in <a href="#eq:fdd-products" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fdd-products">[eq:fdd-products]</a> to the limit. The resulting expression is the same product expectation for $B^h$: its semigroup is $e^{-sG}$ and its initial law $\mu_h$ is stationary because $G1=0$. Products of continuous functions determine the laws on each finite product of the compact sphere. Repeated observation times can be combined into a single test function, so all finite-dimensional distributions converge. ◻

</details>

<a id="eq:resolvent-functions"></a>
<a id="eq:resolvent-graph-convergence"></a>
We shall also need the operator applied to a resolvent approximation. If $f\in C(S^2)$, $g_n=f\circ z_n$, and $$\label{eq:resolvent-functions}
 u_{n,\alpha}=\alpha(\alpha+G_n)^{-1}g_n,
 \qquad u_\alpha=\alpha(\alpha+G)^{-1}f,$$ then, for every fixed $\alpha>0$, $$\label{eq:resolvent-graph-convergence}
 J_nu_{n,\alpha}\longrightarrow Ju_\alpha,
 \qquad J_nG_nu_{n,\alpha}\longrightarrow JGu_\alpha
 \quad\hbox{in }H.$$ The first assertion follows from <a href="#eq:resolvent-convergence" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:resolvent-convergence">[eq:resolvent-convergence]</a> and $J_ng_n\to Jf$. The second follows from the resolvent equations $G_nu_{n,\alpha}=\alpha(g_n-u_{n,\alpha})$ and $Gu_\alpha=\alpha(f-u_\alpha)$.

<a id="sec:tightness"></a>
### From energy recovery to path tightness

Finite-dimensional convergence does not control excursions between observation times. We obtain that control from the local energy recovery in Proposition <a href="#prop:inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:inputs">4</a>. First we extend buffered cutoff recoveries to the whole graph. A reversible maximal inequality then makes their resolvent approximations accurate along the entire stationary path. The bounded generators of those approximations will control motion after stopping times.

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### Uniform recovery and a stationary maximal inequality

<a id="stmt-lem-uniform-recovery"></a>
<div id="lem:uniform-recovery" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:uniform-recovery"></a>
**Lemma 6** (Uniform cutoff recovery). *Let $\chi:S^2\to[0,1]$ be smooth, and put $f=1-\chi$. Suppose $\operatorname{supp}\chi\subset V\Subset W\Subset U$ for buffered conformal chart regions to which the local recovery statement of Proposition <a href="#prop:inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:inputs">4</a> applies. There are real vertex functions $w_n$ such that $$\label{eq:uniform-recovery}
 \lVert w_n-f\circ z_n\rVert_{\infty,V_n}\longrightarrow0,
 \qquad \limsup_n\mathcal E_n(w_n)\le\mathcal E_h(f).$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-uniform-recovery"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-uniform-recovery</summary>

*Proof.* Apply local recovery to $\chi$ on $U$, obtaining $\widetilde\chi_n$ uniformly close to $\chi\circ z_n$ at vertices in $W$. Choose $e_n\downarrow0$ bounding these uniform errors; decreasingness can be achieved by taking tail suprema. Define $$\chi_n(v)=
 \begin{cases}
 \min\{1,\max\{0,\widetilde\chi_n(v)-e_n\}\},&z_n(v)\in W,\\
 0,&z_n(v)\notin W,
 \end{cases}
 \qquad w_n=1-\chi_n.$$ The clipping map is $1$-Lipschitz, and $\chi$ vanishes outside $W$, so $\lVert\chi_n-\chi\circ z_n\rVert_\infty\le2e_n$. More importantly, $\chi_n(v)>0$ implies $\chi(z_n(v))>0$. Thus an edge with a nonzero difference of $\chi_n$ has an endpoint in $\operatorname{supp}\chi\Subset V$. The vanishing edge mesh and the buffer ensure that every such edge is eventually wholly in $W$, with both endpoints there. Its difference is then bounded in absolute value by the corresponding difference of $\widetilde\chi_n$. Local recovery therefore gives $$\limsup_n\mathcal E_n(w_n)
 =\limsup_n\frac12\sum_e(d_e\chi_n)^2
 \le\frac12\int_U\lvert\nabla\chi\rvert^2\,\mathrm d\mathrm{vol}
 =\mathcal E_h(f).$$ The last equality uses the support condition and conformal invariance of the two-dimensional Dirichlet integral. This proves <a href="#eq:uniform-recovery" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:uniform-recovery">[eq:uniform-recovery]</a>, including the factor $1/2$. ◻

</details>

The following estimate is a finite-state analogue of the forward–backward martingale argument of Lyons and Zheng (Lyons and Zheng 1988) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[LyonsZheng1988](#ref-LyonsZheng1988)]. We include its proof to handle jumps explicitly and to make the uniformity in the chain clear.

<a id="stmt-lem-stationary-maximal"></a>
<div id="lem:stationary-maximal" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:stationary-maximal"></a>
**Lemma 7** (Stationary maximal inequality). *Let $Y$ be a continuous-time Markov chain on a finite state space, with generator $-A$ and reversible probability measure $\pi$, started from $\pi$. For every real function $v$ on that space and every $T>0$, $$\label{eq:stationary-maximal}
 \mathbb E\sup_{0\le t\le T}\lvert v(Y_t)\rvert^2
 \le 2\lVert v\rVert_{L^2(\pi)}^2+40T\langle v,Av\rangle_{L^2(\pi)}.$$ The constants do not depend on the state space or its jump rates.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-stationary-maximal"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-stationary-maximal</summary>

*Proof.* Write $q(x,y)$ for the off-diagonal jump rates. The process $$N_t=v(Y_t)-v(Y_0)+\int_0^t Av(Y_s)\,\mathrm ds$$ is a square-integrable martingale starting from zero. Its quadratic variation and stationarity give $$\begin{aligned}
 \mathbb E\lvert N_T\rvert^2
 &=T\sum_x\pi(x)\sum_{y\ne x}q(x,y)(v(y)-v(x))^2\\
 &=2T\langle v,Av\rangle_{L^2(\pi)}.
\end{aligned}$$ Over $[0,T]$, reverse the path using its right-continuous modification in reversed time. Reversibility and stationarity give the same chain law, so its corresponding martingale $N'$ satisfies the same second-moment identity. At every fixed $t\in[0,T]$, almost surely there is no jump at $t$ or at either deterministic endpoint. Subtracting the forward and backward formulas yields $$2\bigl(v(Y_t)-v(Y_0)\bigr)=N_t-(N'_T-N'_{T-t}).$$ Use this identity first at a countable dense set of times, together with $T$, and then use right continuity. It follows that $$\sup_{t\le T}\lvert v(Y_t)\rvert
 \le \lvert v(Y_0)\rvert+\frac12\sup_{t\le T}\lvert N_t\rvert
                       +\sup_{t\le T}\lvert N'_t\rvert.$$ If the two martingale suprema are $a$ and $b$, the square of the right side is at most $2\lvert v(Y_0)\rvert^2+a^2+4b^2$. Doob’s $L^2$ inequality bounds each of $\mathbb Ea^2$ and $\mathbb Eb^2$ by $8T\langle v,Av\rangle_{L^2(\pi)}$, proving <a href="#eq:stationary-maximal" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:stationary-maximal">[eq:stationary-maximal]</a>. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### Resolvent approximation along the whole path

<a id="stmt-lem-resolvent-approximation"></a>
<div id="lem:resolvent-approximation" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:pathwise-resolvent-approximation"></a>
<a id="eq:bounded-generator"></a>
**Lemma 8**. *For a cutoff $f=1-\chi$ as in Lemma <a href="#lem:uniform-recovery" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:uniform-recovery">6</a>, write $g_n=f\circ z_n$ and define $u_{n,\alpha}$ and $u_\alpha$ by <a href="#eq:resolvent-functions" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:resolvent-functions">[eq:resolvent-functions]</a>. For every $T>0$, $$\label{eq:pathwise-resolvent-approximation}
 \lim_{\alpha\to\infty}\limsup_n
 \mathbb E\sup_{0\le t\le T}
       \lvert u_{n,\alpha}(Y^n_t)-g_n(Y^n_t)\rvert=0.$$ For every $n$ and $\alpha>0$, moreover, $$\label{eq:bounded-generator}
 0\le u_{n,\alpha}\le1,
 \qquad \lVert G_nu_{n,\alpha}\rVert_\infty\le\alpha.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-resolvent-approximation"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-resolvent-approximation</summary>

<a id="eq:form-norm-approximation"></a>
*Proof.* Take the recoveries $w_n$ from Lemma <a href="#lem:uniform-recovery" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:uniform-recovery">6</a>. Their uniform approximation, together with the tour convergence, gives $J_nw_n\to Jf$ in $H$. For fixed $\alpha$, Equation <a href="#eq:resolvent-graph-convergence" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:resolvent-graph-convergence">[eq:resolvent-graph-convergence]</a> therefore implies $$\begin{aligned}
 &\limsup_n\bigl(
   \lVert w_n-u_{n,\alpha}\rVert_{L^2(\mu_n)}^2
       +\mathcal E_n(w_n-u_{n,\alpha})\bigr)\notag\\
 &\hspace{15mm}\le
   \lVert f-u_\alpha\rVert_{L^2(\mu_h)}^2+\mathcal E_h(f-u_\alpha).
 \label{eq:form-norm-approximation}
\end{aligned}$$ To see the energy assertion explicitly, expand $$\mathcal E_n(w_n-u_{n,\alpha})
 =\mathcal E_n(w_n)-2\langle w_n,G_nu_{n,\alpha}\rangle_{L^2(\mu_n)}
            +\langle u_{n,\alpha},G_nu_{n,\alpha}\rangle_{L^2(\mu_n)}.$$ The last two terms converge by <a href="#eq:resolvent-graph-convergence" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:resolvent-graph-convergence">[eq:resolvent-graph-convergence]</a>, and the first has limsup at most $\mathcal E_h(f)$. Since $u_\alpha$ lies in the operator domain of $G$, the resulting expression is the form expansion of $\mathcal E_h(f-u_\alpha)$.

The right side of <a href="#eq:form-norm-approximation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:form-norm-approximation">[eq:form-norm-approximation]</a> tends to zero. Indeed, if $\nu_f$ is the spectral measure of $G$ at $f$, then it equals $$\int_{[0,\infty)}(1+\lambda)
       \left(\frac{\lambda}{\alpha+\lambda}\right)^2\,\mathrm d\nu_f(\lambda).$$ Here $f$ belongs to the form domain, so $1+\lambda$ is integrable against $\nu_f$; dominated convergence applies. Lemma <a href="#lem:stationary-maximal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:stationary-maximal">7</a>, applied with $A=G_n$ to $w_n-u_{n,\alpha}$, now gives $$\lim_{\alpha\to\infty}\limsup_n
 \mathbb E\sup_{t\le T}\lvert w_n(Y^n_t)-u_{n,\alpha}(Y^n_t)\rvert^2=0.$$ Cauchy–Schwarz and the uniform bound on $w_n-g_n$ yield <a href="#eq:pathwise-resolvent-approximation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pathwise-resolvent-approximation">[eq:pathwise-resolvent-approximation]</a>.

Finally, the Markov semigroup representation $$u_{n,\alpha}=\int_0^\infty\alpha e^{-\alpha t}
                              e^{-tG_n}g_n\,\mathrm dt$$ shows that $0\le u_{n,\alpha}\le1$. The resolvent identity $G_nu_{n,\alpha}=\alpha(g_n-u_{n,\alpha})$ proves the remaining bound in <a href="#eq:bounded-generator" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:bounded-generator">[eq:bounded-generator]</a>. ◻

</details>

We now have approximations accurate for all times of the stationary path, not merely at a fixed time. Their generator bound makes that accuracy useful even when the starting time of an increment is selected from the path.

<a id="pm-h-13"></a>
#### Stopping-time increments

<a id="stmt-prop-path-limit"></a>
<div id="prop:path-limit" class="proposition">

**Proposition 9**. *On every fixed realization supplied by Proposition <a href="#prop:inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:inputs">4</a>, and for every $T>0$, $$(\xi^n_t)_{0\le t\le T}\ \Longrightarrow\
 (B^h_t)_{0\le t\le T}
 \quad\hbox{in }D([0,T],S^2)$$ for the Skorokhod $J_1$ topology associated with the round metric $\rho$. Every subsequential path limit is continuous.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-path-limit"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-path-limit</summary>

<a id="eq:separating-cutoffs"></a>
*Proof.* Fix $d>0$. Choose finitely many round disks $O_1,\ldots,O_m$ covering $S^2$ and smooth cutoffs $f_i=1-\chi_i$ to which Lemma <a href="#lem:uniform-recovery" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:uniform-recovery">6</a> applies, such that $$\label{eq:separating-cutoffs}
 f_i=0\text{ on }O_i,
 \qquad x\in O_i,\ \rho(x,y)\ge d\ \Longrightarrow\ f_i(y)=1.$$ For example, take sufficiently small inner disks and slightly larger support disks, all compactly contained in chart regions and all with diameter smaller than $d$. Compactness gives a finite subcover, and there is room for the recovery buffers outside each support disk.

Let $\tau,\sigma$ be stopping times for $Y^n$, with $0\le\tau\le\sigma\le T$ and $\sigma-\tau\le s$. Let $I$ be the first index for which $\xi^n_\tau\in O_I$. This index is measurable with respect to the information $\mathcal F_\tau$ at time $\tau$. Since $0\le f_i\le1$, <a href="#eq:separating-cutoffs" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:separating-cutoffs">[eq:separating-cutoffs]</a> implies $$\mathbb P\{\rho(\xi^n_\tau,\xi^n_\sigma)\ge d\}
 \le\mathbb E\bigl[f_I(\xi^n_\sigma)-f_I(\xi^n_\tau)\bigr].$$ Denote by $u^{(i)}_{n,\alpha}$ the resolvent approximation of $f_i\circ z_n$. Optional sampling of its generator martingale gives $$\begin{aligned}
 &\mathbb E\bigl[u^{(I)}_{n,\alpha}(Y^n_\sigma)
                -u^{(I)}_{n,\alpha}(Y^n_\tau)\bigr]\\
 &\qquad=-\sum_{i=1}^m\mathbb E\left[
    \mathbf1_{\{I=i\}}\int_\tau^\sigma
                    G_nu^{(i)}_{n,\alpha}(Y^n_t)\,\mathrm dt\right]
 \le\alpha s.
\end{aligned}$$ The equality is valid because each event $\{I=i\}$ lies in $\mathcal F_\tau$; no independence of $I$ and the path is required. The last inequality uses <a href="#eq:bounded-generator" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:bounded-generator">[eq:bounded-generator]</a> and the fact that the events $\{I=i\}$ partition the sample space. Consequently <a id="eq:aldous-bound"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 \mathbb P\{\rho(\xi^n_\tau,\xi^n_\sigma)\ge d\}
 &\le\alpha s+2\sum_{i=1}^m
 \mathbb E\sup_{0\le t\le T}
   \lvert f_i(\xi^n_t)-u^{(i)}_{n,\alpha}(Y^n_t)\rvert.
 \label{eq:aldous-bound}
\end{aligned}$$ This bound is uniform over all the stated stopping-time pairs. First take $\limsup_n$, then let $s\downarrow0$ with $\alpha$ fixed, and finally let $\alpha\to\infty$. Lemma <a href="#lem:resolvent-approximation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:resolvent-approximation">8</a> gives $$\lim_{s\downarrow0}\limsup_n
 \sup_{\substack{\tau\le\sigma\le T\\0\le\sigma-\tau\le s}}
 \mathbb P\{\rho(\xi^n_\tau,\xi^n_\sigma)\ge d\}=0.$$ This is the stopping-time increment condition in Aldous’s tightness criterion (Aldous 1978) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[Aldous1978](#ref-Aldous1978)]. Compact containment is automatic on $S^2$, so the laws are tight in $D([0,T],S^2)$ with its $J_1$ topology.

Every jump of $\xi^n$ joins the endpoints of an embedded map edge. Proposition <a href="#prop:inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:inputs">4</a> therefore makes its largest jump tend to zero. A jump of a $J_1$ limit must be approximated by jumps of comparable size; hence every subsequential limit is continuous. Evaluation at fixed times is continuous at continuous paths, so Proposition <a href="#prop:operators" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:operators">5</a> identifies the finite-dimensional laws of every such limit with those of $B^h$. These finite-dimensional laws determine the continuous path law. Tightness and uniqueness of the subsequential limit prove the proposition. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:paths"></a>
### Cable paths and the joint limit

Proposition <a href="#prop:path-limit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:path-limit">9</a> identifies the walk in conformal coordinates and with exponential holding times. We now place the original cable graphs and the limiting metric sphere in one compact space, replace the exponential clock by the deterministic clock, and restore the randomness of the environment. Until the final proof, the realization supplied by Proposition <a href="#prop:inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:inputs">4</a> remains fixed.

<a id="pm-h-15"></a>
#### A common metric space

The following construction uses only the vertex-distance comparison. In particular, it does not require an extension of the conformal coordinate map to the interiors of cable edges.

<a id="stmt-lem-ambient-space"></a>
<div id="lem:ambient-space" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:vertex-bridges"></a>
<a id="eq:cable-hausdorff"></a>
**Lemma 10** (A compact ambient space). *Let $C_n$ be finite connected cable graphs, with vertex sets $V_n$ and all edge lengths equal to $a_n>0$, where $a_n\to0$. Write $d_{C_n}$ for their path metrics. Let $(S,d)$ be a compact metric space, and suppose that maps $z_n:V_n\to S$ satisfy $$\begin{aligned}
 \delta_n&:=\sup_{u,v\in V_n}
  \lvert d_{C_n}(u,v)-d(z_n(u),z_n(v))\rvert\longrightarrow0,\\
 \varepsilon_n&:=d_{\mathrm H}^{S}(z_n(V_n),S)\longrightarrow0.
\end{aligned}$$ There is a compact metric space $(Z,d_Z)$ containing isometric copies of $S$ and every $C_n$, such that, with $c_n=\delta_n/2+a_n$, $$\begin{aligned}
 \sup_{v\in V_n}d_Z(v,z_n(v))&\le c_n,
 \label{eq:vertex-bridges}\\
 d_{\mathrm H}^{Z}(C_n,S)&\le
  \max\{a_n+c_n,\varepsilon_n+c_n\}.
 \label{eq:cable-hausdorff}
\end{aligned}$$ If $\nu_n$ is a probability measure on $V_n$ and $(z_n)_*\nu_n\Rightarrow\nu$ on $S$, then $d_{\mathrm P}^{Z}(\nu_n,\nu)\to0$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-ambient-space"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-ambient-space</summary>

*Proof.* Start with the disjoint union of $S$ and the $C_n$. Retain the metric within each piece, and allow a crossing from $v\in V_n$ to $z_n(v)\in S$ at cost $c_n$. Define distance as the infimum of the costs of finite chains made of such crossings and movements within individual pieces.

This construction does not shorten any of the original metrics. Indeed, an excursion from $S$ through $C_n$, entering at $z_n(u)$ and returning at $z_n(v)$, costs at least $$2c_n+d_{C_n}(u,v)\ge d(z_n(u),z_n(v)).$$ Replacing all such excursions by movements in $S$ proves the assertion for pairs of points of $S$. For points of a fixed $C_n$, first remove excursions through the other cable graphs in this way. Each remaining excursion out of $C_n$, from a vertex $u$ to a vertex $v$, costs at least $$2c_n+d(z_n(u),z_n(v))\ge d_{C_n}(u,v),$$ so it too can be removed. This also proves the assertion for points in edge interiors. Distinct pieces remain separated: every chain leaving $C_n$ pays at least the positive cost $c_n$. Thus the chain construction defines a metric, not merely a pseudometric.

Every point of $C_n$ is at distance at most $a_n$ from a vertex, even when its edge is a loop. It is consequently at distance at most $a_n+c_n$ from $S$. Conversely, every point of $S$ is at distance at most $\varepsilon_n+c_n$ from $C_n$. These observations prove <a href="#eq:vertex-bridges" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:vertex-bridges">[eq:vertex-bridges]</a>–<a href="#eq:cable-hausdorff" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cable-hausdorff">[eq:cable-hausdorff]</a> on the union. The union is totally bounded: its tail lies arbitrarily close to the compact space $S$, and each of the finitely many remaining cable graphs is compact. Its completion is the required compact space $Z$.

The coupling $v\mapsto(v,z_n(v))$ of $\nu_n$ and $(z_n)_*\nu_n$ has all its mass at pairs at distance at most $c_n$, whence $$d_{\mathrm P}^{Z}(\nu_n,\nu)
 \le c_n+d_{\mathrm P}^{Z}((z_n)_*\nu_n,\nu)\longrightarrow0.$$ For the last convergence, weak convergence on the compact subspace $S$ is also weak convergence in $Z$. ◻

</details>

Apply Lemma <a href="#lem:ambient-space" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:ambient-space">10</a> to $C_n=(\overline M_n,a_nd_n)$ and $(S,d)=(S^2,D_h)$, with $\nu_n=\mu_n$. The hypotheses are precisely the metric and measure conclusions of Proposition <a href="#prop:inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:inputs">4</a>; vertex distances in a cable graph equal the corresponding graph distances. We henceforth regard these spaces as subsets of the resulting $Z$.

<a id="eq:ambient-path-limit"></a>
Because $\rho$ and $D_h$ induce the same topology on the compact sphere, the identity map between the two metric spheres is uniformly continuous in both directions. Applying the same time changes in the definition of the Skorokhod $J_1$ topology therefore transfers Proposition <a href="#prop:path-limit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:path-limit">9</a> from $\rho$ to $D_h$. Moreover, <a href="#eq:vertex-bridges" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:vertex-bridges">[eq:vertex-bridges]</a> gives the deterministic bound $$\sup_{0\le t\le T}d_Z(Y^n_t,\xi^n_t)\le c_n.$$ It follows that, for every $T<\infty$, $$\label{eq:ambient-path-limit}
 Y^n\ \Longrightarrow\ B^h
 \quad\text{in }D([0,T],Z)\text{ with the }J_1\text{ topology}.$$

<a id="eq:continuous-limit-modulus"></a>
We shall use one consequence of the continuity of $B^h$. For a path $y:[0,T]\to Z$, set $$\omega_T(y,s)=
 \sup_{\substack{t,t'\in[0,T]\\\lvert t-t'\rvert\le s}}
    d_Z(y_t,y_{t'}).$$ Then, for every $\eta>0$, $$\label{eq:continuous-limit-modulus}
 \lim_{s\downarrow0}\limsup_n
 \mathbb P\bigl[\omega_T(Y^n,s)>\eta\bigr]=0.$$ To see this directly, use the Skorokhod representation theorem (Billingsley 1999, Theorem 6.7) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Billingsley1999](#ref-Billingsley1999)] on the Polish space $D([0,T],Z)$ in <a href="#eq:ambient-path-limit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ambient-path-limit">[eq:ambient-path-limit]</a>. Convergence in $J_1$ to a continuous path is uniform: composing with the convergent time changes changes that limiting path uniformly little; see also (Billingsley 1999, sec. 12, p. 124) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Billingsley1999](#ref-Billingsley1999)]. On this representation, $$\omega_T(Y^n,s)\le
 2\sup_{t\le T}d_Z(Y^n_t,B^h_t)+\omega_T(B^h,s).$$ Uniform continuity of each limiting path proves <a href="#eq:continuous-limit-modulus" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:continuous-limit-modulus">[eq:continuous-limit-modulus]</a>. The resulting probability statement depends only on the original path laws, not on the chosen representation.

<a id="pm-h-16"></a>
#### Removing the exponential clock

The clock comparison must count every attempted step, including a loop traversal that does not change the vertex of the continuous-time chain.

<a id="stmt-lem-clock"></a>
<div id="lem:clock" class="lemma">

<a id="eq:clock-comparison"></a>
**Lemma 11** (The attempt clock). *Fix a realization supplied by Proposition <a href="#prop:inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:inputs">4</a>, for which <a href="#eq:ambient-path-limit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ambient-path-limit">[eq:ambient-path-limit]</a> holds. Let $X^n$ be the stationary discrete walk on $M_n$, including its selected half-edges, and let $\overline X^n$ be its cable interpolation. Independently of the walk, let $(e_{n,k})_{k\ge1}$ be independent exponential random variables of mean $1/n$, and put $$S_{n,0}=0,\qquad S_{n,k}=\sum_{j=1}^k e_{n,j},\qquad
 N_n(t)=\max\{k:S_{n,k}\le t\}.$$ Then $Y^n_t=X^n_{N_n(t)}$ has generator $-G_n$ and $Y^n_{S_{n,k}}=X^n_k$. In the ambient space of Lemma <a href="#lem:ambient-space" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:ambient-space">10</a>, for every $T<\infty$, $$\label{eq:clock-comparison}
 \sup_{0\le t\le T}
 d_Z(\overline X^n_{nt},Y^n_t)\longrightarrow0
 \quad\text{in probability}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-clock"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-clock</summary>

*Proof.* Attempts occur at rate $n$ and apply the transition kernel $P_n$, so the generator is $n(P_n-I)=-G_n$. This remains true when an attempt traverses a loop. The identity at $S_{n,k}$ follows from the right-continuous definition of $N_n$.

<a id="eq:clock-deviation"></a>
Fix $T$ and write $$\Delta_{n,T}=
 \max_{0\le k\le\lfloor nT\rfloor}\lvert S_{n,k}-k/n\rvert.$$ The centered partial sums form a martingale. Since $\operatorname{Var}(e_{n,k})=n^{-2}$, the $L^2$ maximal inequality gives $$\label{eq:clock-deviation}
 \mathbb E[\Delta_{n,T}^2]
 \le \frac{4\lfloor nT\rfloor}{n^2}\longrightarrow0.$$ For $k=\lfloor nt\rfloor$, the point $\overline X^n_{nt}$ lies on the cable edge traversed from $X^n_k$, at distance at most $a_n$ from that vertex. Thus $$d_Z(\overline X^n_{nt},Y^n_t)
 \le a_n+d_Z(Y^n_{S_{n,k}},Y^n_t),
 \qquad
 \lvert S_{n,k}-t\rvert\le\Delta_{n,T}+1/n.$$ The spatial bound holds for loop edges as well as nonloop edges. On the event $\Delta_{n,T}+1/n\le s\le1$, all the times appearing here lie in $[0,T+1]$. Consequently, for $a_n<\eta/2$, $$\mathbb P\left[\sup_{t\le T}
 d_Z(\overline X^n_{nt},Y^n_t)>\eta\right]
 \le \mathbb P[\Delta_{n,T}+1/n>s]
   +\mathbb P[\omega_{T+1}(Y^n,s)>\eta/2].$$ First take the upper limit in $n$, using <a href="#eq:clock-deviation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:clock-deviation">[eq:clock-deviation]</a>, and then let $s\downarrow0$, using <a href="#eq:continuous-limit-modulus" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:continuous-limit-modulus">[eq:continuous-limit-modulus]</a> on $[0,T+1]$. This proves <a href="#eq:clock-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:clock-comparison">[eq:clock-comparison]</a>. ◻

</details>

Combining Lemma <a href="#lem:clock" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:clock">11</a> and <a href="#eq:ambient-path-limit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:ambient-path-limit">[eq:ambient-path-limit]</a> gives convergence of the cable paths to $B^h$ in $D([0,T],Z)$. Both the cable paths and the limiting paths are continuous. The continuous path space is closed in the $J_1$ topology: a convergent sequence of continuous paths, after the defining time changes, converges uniformly and hence has a continuous limit. Its induced topology is the uniform topology, by the same time-change argument used above. Thus in fact <a id="eq:cable-finite-horizon"></a>$$\label{eq:cable-finite-horizon}
 (\overline X^n_{nt})_{0\le t\le T}
 \ \Longrightarrow\ (B^h_t)_{0\le t\le T}
 \quad\text{in }C([0,T],Z).$$

<a id="eq:cable-local-limit"></a>
This convergence holds on every integer time horizon, and therefore also in $C([0,\infty),Z)$ with the topology of uniform convergence on compact time intervals. Here are the compactness details. For each integer $m\ge1$ and $\epsilon>0$, finite-horizon tightness supplies a compact set $K_m\subset C([0,m],Z)$ such that the probability of leaving $K_m$ is at most $\epsilon2^{-m}$, uniformly in $n$. The set of paths whose restriction belongs to $K_m$ for every $m$ is compact in $C([0,\infty),Z)$, by a diagonal subsequence argument, and has probability at least $1-\epsilon$. Every subsequential limit has, by the continuous restriction maps, the law of $B^h$ on every $[0,m]$. These restrictions determine the entire path law. We have proved $$\label{eq:cable-local-limit}
 (\overline X^n_{nt})_{t\ge0}
 \ \Longrightarrow\ (B^h_t)_{t\ge0}
 \quad\text{in }C([0,\infty),Z).$$

<a id="pm-h-17"></a>
#### Restoring the random environment

We have obtained the conditional path limit for every fixed realization in the probability-one event of Proposition <a href="#prop:inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:inputs">4</a>. We now combine it with convergence of the underlying measured spaces.

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-clock-2"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-clock</summary>

*Proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.* <span id="proofmain" label="proofmain"></span> Start from an arbitrary subsequence and take the coupled further subsequence of Proposition <a href="#prop:inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:inputs">4</a>. Fix a realization in its probability-one event and a finite integer $r\ge1$. Conditional on this realization, the $r$ cable paths are independent with the common law in <a href="#eq:cable-local-limit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cable-local-limit">[eq:cable-local-limit]</a>. Their joint law therefore converges to that of $r$ independent copies of $B^h$ in $C([0,\infty),Z)^r$. For example, a Skorokhod representation of <a href="#eq:cable-local-limit" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:cable-local-limit">[eq:cable-local-limit]</a>, repeated on $r$ independent probability spaces, couples the product laws with almost sure convergence of every coordinate.

Lemma <a href="#lem:ambient-space" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:ambient-space">10</a> also gives Hausdorff convergence of the spaces and Prokhorov convergence of their measures in the same $Z$. Under the preceding product coupling, the curves converge uniformly on every compact time interval. By the definition of the curve-decorated Gromov–Hausdorff–Prokhorov topology, the decorated spaces consequently converge almost surely under that coupling. In particular, for any bounded continuous function $F$ on the space of such decorated objects, the expectation of $F$ over the $r$ walks on this fixed environment converges to its expectation over $r$ independent stationary Liouville Brownian motions on the fixed limiting sphere.

These expectations are the conditional expectations obtained by sampling walks with fresh randomness after the environment coupling. The walk kernel on a finite graph depends only on the map, not on the auxiliary coordinates, marks, or tours used in that coupling; the limiting kernel is the diffusion law specified by the quantum sphere. We have proved pointwise convergence of these conditional expectations on the probability-one event. The compact space $Z$, the path couplings, and the recovery functions used earlier are witnesses for this pointwise statement. They need not be chosen measurably as the environment varies. The conditional expectations from these sampling kernels are measurable, and bounded convergence now gives convergence of the unconditional expectations of $F$ along the coupled further subsequence.

Thus every subsequence admits a further subsequence with the asserted joint limit. This proves convergence along the full sequence, for each fixed $r$. Lemma <a href="#lem:clock" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:clock">11</a> used the deterministic acceleration $b_n=n$, which has exactly the time normalization in the theorem. ◻

</details>
